Company - Competitive advantage

❌ D467 questions
C-4148C-4232C-4370C-4458C-4538C-4640C-4745

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Hexatronic's competitive advantage is real, narrow and niche-bound: it wins on installation economics, local compliant capacity and certifications, which lets it outgrow peers in the US upswing and hold defence and subsea niches, but it has no scale, no upstream position and no demonstrated real pricing power, and its advantages must be re-earned each period through lead times, training and product releases rather than compounding mechanically. The addressable market is large enough that growth is constrained by supply, labour and execution, not by demand. The modelling implication is that Hexatronic should be treated as a cyclical niche specialist with a mix tailwind: margins can reach the low teens in a strong year (group adjusted EBITA about 11-12%) but should not be modelled at peer-leading levels, pricing should be modelled as cost pass-through with a lag, and share gains in the US should be assumed to fade once the integrated majors' capacity arrives.

C-41

Which sustainable relative competitive advantages or moats does the company hold (customer-related; production-related; scale-based; regulatory or political; culture or knowledge), how strong is each, and are they widening or narrowing?

❌ D48

Judgement. Hexatronic holds real but narrow moats, none of them scale-based and none upstream: a customer-related moat in blown-fibre system selling (moderate, stable), a production moat in local, compliant plants (moderate, widening in the US), a regulatory moat in BABA eligibility and defence certifications (moderate, widening), and a culture-and-knowledge edge in installer relationships and responsiveness (modest, untested at scale). Scale is a disadvantage that is widening as the majors consolidate. The advantages earn a niche margin in an upswing; they do not compound.

  • Customer-related (system selling): patented air-blown systems (Stingray, Viper) in which ducts and cables must interoperate; installer training, certification and system warranties create practical exclusivity; labour is about 80% of network project cost, so installation speed outweighs component price. Quantified utility: Viper Ease cuts preparation time 50-70%; Viper Core blows over 3,000m and cuts splicing 33%. Strength moderate; stable in the US and among alt-nets; narrowing in Europe, where price wins in commoditised duct.
  • Production (local plants): 18 plants, four in the US, all US ducts made locally; short lead times; Clinton capacity retained and the South Carolina cable line due in 2027. Widening in the US (BABA, tariffs, scarcity).
  • Scale: low (about 1% US share); narrowing relative to Corning, Prysmian and Amphenol.
  • Regulatory/political: BABA eligibility for ducts (cable from 2027); defence certifications with 8-10-year lifecycles and MIL-TAC standards; German FDI clearance obtained for JOWO. Widening with defence budgets.
  • Culture/knowledge: decentralised, founder-led acquired units; expert calls credit speed, training centres and value-added services that support a price premium; untested against large-scale integration.
  • What is missing: glass and preform, high-density data-centre IP, standards influence (the multicore standard was set by Corning, Sumitomo, AFL and TeraHop).
C-42

How large is the company's scale relative to competitors and other actors (revenue, headcount and so on), and what does that scale confer?

❌ D32

Judgement. Hexatronic is small relative to the competitors and suppliers that matter, and its scale confers only regional density and an acquisition platform; it confers no purchasing power over glass, no research scale for standards, and a fragility as a small-capitalisation stock.

  • Size: revenue about SEK 8.6bn (2026E) against Prysmian EUR 19.7bn, Nexans EUR 7.8bn, NKT EUR 2.7bn, Corning Optical Communications about USD 8bn annualised; employees 2,133 against Prysmian's 33,000 and NKT's 6,600; market capitalisation about SEK 8.6-8.8bn with about USD 7m of daily value traded.
  • What scale gives: enough density to run 18 plants and local supply in the US, Nordics, UK and Germany; enough cash flow and balance sheet to buy small service companies at 5-7x.
  • What it does not give: bargaining power with concentrated glass suppliers (hence the current allocation risk); the ability to fund research and standards at the pace of the majors; resilience to sector-wide sentiment shocks.
C-43

How large is the realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and what rate of growth does it allow for over the mid term?

C70

Judgement. The realistic addressable market is very large relative to Hexatronic, so market size is not the constraint on growth over the mid term; supply, labour, execution and capital are. A realistic mid-term growth allowance is about 7-10% a year organically for the group (Data Center 15-20%, Harsh Environment 6-10%, US Fiber Solutions mid-to-high single digits, Europe flat to slightly down) plus 3-5% a year from acquisitions, giving roughly 10-14% reported growth before currency.

  • Data centre: global capacity 97 GW (2024) to 226 GW (2030); data-centre optical-cable demand about 20% a year 2025-31, slowing from 2028; the US data-centre market is about six times Europe's capacity. Hexatronic's Data Center revenue of about SEK 2.2bn in 2026 is a small share.
  • US fibre: BEAD USD 42.45bn plus private builds (about 80% of US fibre construction); BEAD expected to lift total US optical-fibre demand 5-10% a year once construction ramps.
  • Europe: shrinking new-build market, growing connection market; UK about 70% passed; Germany and UK coverage gaps give years of steady demand at lower volumes.
  • Defence and subsea: drone fibre now about 15% of global fibre demand; subsea demand growth 25% a year (North America) and 15-18% (Europe) over five years (Meta procurement expert).
C-44

How high is the company's surplus customer utility against competing or alternative offerings, and is it leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior? (Hard to prove, easier to disprove; the aim is to disprove inferiority at Early-Stage and Deep Dive, for example Sophos, Bytes.)

C58

Judgement. Hexatronic's surplus customer utility is demonstrably not inferior, and probably leading, in installation economics for blown-fibre networks, where it is evidenced by share gains in the US through the downturn and by expert testimony; it is not demonstrated in data-centre services against larger contractors, and it is inferior in high-density data-centre hardware, where it lacks the IP. In European commodity duct, its utility advantage has not been enough to resist price competition.

  • Evidence for: labour-saving products (50-70% shorter preparation; 33% fewer splices; long blowing distances); customers' installation-labour constraint makes these features valuable; a claimed 20-30% total-cost-of-ownership premium; expert calls (Ting Internet, Blue Ridge, a competitor's strategy manager) praise speed, design and training; Clinton reversal and North American growth above peers.
  • Evidence against or missing: an expert questions its ability to scale against Corning; Data Center services quality relative to Dycom-type contractors is unproven; European volume losses in price wars; no published customer satisfaction or win-rate data.
C-45

To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?

❌ D38

Judgement. Pricing power is not demonstrated in real terms. The evidence of application is nominal, cost-driven and industry-wide pass-through in the US (2026), price given back in the US in 2024-25 and in Europe throughout, and a gross margin that has been protected rather than expanded by price. Latent real pricing exists in scarce segments, but there is no evidence of 2% a year real increases with stable customer utility.

  • Applied: 2026 US price increases with the industry; Q3 2026 price increases for fibre and transport costs; index-linked clauses with about a three-month lag.
  • Given back: US duct prices lower year on year in 2024-25 (offset by volume); European microduct prices down with volume and overcapacity.
  • Outcome: adjusted gross margin 41.4% (Q4 2024), 40.1% (Q2 2025), 37.5% (Q3 and Q4 2025), 38.7% (Q2 2026), 39.8% (Q3 2026): recovering, not expanding past prior levels.
  • Assessments: Redeye sees limited ability to raise prices above inflation without churn; management withholds magnitudes.
C-46

To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto, in its messaging, strategy, culture or values?

❌ D40

Judgement. There is no evidence that Hexatronic shares scale economies with customers, stated or de facto; its proposition is total cost of ownership through installation savings, not lower prices from scale, and it has little scale to share.

  • Messaging and strategy: focus on easy installation, lead times and service; no statement of passing scale savings to customers; pricing behaviour is cost-plus pass-through.
  • Relevance: the scale-economies-shared pattern (a reinforcing loop of lower prices, more volume, lower unit cost) is not available to a business at about 1% share in its largest market.
C-47

To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?

❌ D45

Judgement. Gravitational forces are weak; most of the improvement in Hexatronic's competitive position and outputs comes from management action and the cycle, not from mechanisms that strengthen it automatically each period. Two mild mechanical forces exist: the installed base of Hexatronic blown-fibre networks pulls through connection and drop products as Europe shifts from passing to connecting, and the portfolio mix lifts the group margin mechanically as the faster-growing, higher-margin segments become a larger share.

  • Installed-base pull-through: networks built with Hexatronic microducts tend to use compatible connection products; Hexatronic UK reports operators shifting purchases from cables to connection products; this cushions Europe but does not grow it.
  • Mix effect: Data Center (15-17% margin) and Harsh Environment (11%, target 15%) growing faster than Fiber Solutions (9-10%) raises the group margin without any change in segment margins; but a large part of that growth is bought.
  • Re-cabling cycle: Redeye argues data centres re-cable every two to three years for new compute generations, giving recurring service work; plausible but unproven in Hexatronic's numbers.
  • Absent: network effects, scale economies, data advantages, contractual lock-in of share.

Company - Economic strength

C5411 questions
C-4966C-5055C-5145C-5245C-5358C-5448C-7558C-7670C-7745C-7862C-7945

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Hexatronic's economics are those of a mid-quality cyclical industrial in a strong upswing: high fixed-cost operating leverage (decremental margins near 100% in 2023-25, incremental margins of about 30% now), moderate margins with a ceiling below the last peak, mediocre returns on capital (about 10-12% post-tax), heavy working capital (about a quarter of sales), low and comfortable financial leverage after the equity raise, and an earnings base that is cleaner than in 2025 but still noisy between adjusted EBITA and EPS. The cost programme is complete, so the next two years' profit growth rests on volume, mix and the few big levers (Harsh Environment margin, Fiber Solutions absorption, subsea from 2028), not on further self-help. For the model: FY2026 adjusted EBITA about SEK 860-890m and clean EPS about SEK 2.25-2.40; 2027 adjusted EBITA about SEK 1.0-1.1bn and EPS about SEK 2.6-3.0, with a wide cone because a 10% swing in Fiber Solutions volume moves EBITA by about 15-20%; tax at 25-27%; amortisation about SEK 100-110m and rising with acquisitions; free cash flow before acquisitions about 60-90% of net income in growth years; no dividend; share count drifting up.

C-49

What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?

C66

Judgement. Hexatronic is in a strong rebound of earnings per share off a depressed 2025 base, with revenue growth of about 14% in 2026 (about half organic), but the rebound rate is not a durable growth rate. On a clean basis FY2026 EPS is likely about SEK 2.25-2.40 (reported about SEK 2.35-2.45 including the Q3 impairment reversal), which is at or slightly above the pre-Q3 consensus of SEK 2.30; the durable rate from 2027 is about 12-18% a year, supported by mix, volume and lower interest, and exposed to normalisation in Data Center and to the US cycle.

  • History: diluted EPS SEK 4.17 (2023), 1.69 (2024), -0.07 (2025 reported; FactSet adjusted 0.80; brokers' basis -0.33). Nine-month 2026 diluted EPS SEK 1.88 (Q1 0.45, Q2 0.61, Q3 0.81).
  • Quality of the Q3 figure: reported EBITA included SEK 71m of net non-recurring effects (a SEK 67m reversal of a Clinton impairment), worth about SEK 0.23 after tax; net financial items included a SEK 28m contingent-consideration revaluation charge (about SEK 0.10). A clean Q3 was therefore about SEK 0.68-0.72.
  • Q4 2026 shape: seasonally lower, Data Center normalising, submarine similar to Q3; adjusted EBITA roughly SEK 210-235m (against SEK 133m in Q4 2025), clean EPS about SEK 0.50-0.58. FY2026 adjusted EBITA about SEK 860-890m (10.0-10.3% margin) on sales of about SEK 8.55-8.7bn.
  • 2027-28: consensus EPS SEK 2.71 and 3.23 (+15-20% a year). A run-rate check supports it with modest upside: H2 2026 adjusted EBITA about SEK 500m (about SEK 1.0bn annualised); 2027 revenue about SEK 9.3-9.6bn at a 10.5-11.5% margin gives adjusted EBITA about SEK 1.0-1.1bn; less amortisation about SEK 100-110m, net financial items about SEK 90-110m, tax about 25%, about 223m shares, gives EPS about SEK 2.6-3.0.
  • Durability: about half of 2026 revenue growth is acquired; 2027 organic growth depends on Data Center sustaining 15-20% on a normalised base and on the US not reversing; the cost programme is complete, so margin progress must come from volume and mix.
C-50

How much immediate scope is there to deploy capital or operating spend at a high return on new invested capital for three to five years, and what does that mean for mid- and long-term potential and the fair multiple?

C55

Judgement. Immediate scope to deploy capital at a high return is moderate: about 5-10% of sales a year can be deployed (small acquisitions plus one subsea project) at mid-teens returns, not the 20%-plus of sales at 25-30% returns that supports a compounder multiple. This argues for a mid-teens fair price-to-earnings multiple in the mid term, with upside only if acquisitions prove able to lift acquired margins and Data Center re-cabling proves recurring.

  • Organic: maintenance capex 1.5-3% of sales (Q3 2026 1.5%); the Hudiksvall subsea line (Redeye estimate about SEK 300m for about SEK 325m of sales at a 20% EBITA margin, implying about 16-20% pre-tax return on the capex, operational 2028); the South Carolina cable line in 2027. Capex consensus SEK 163m / 264m / 279m for 2026-28.
  • Acquisitions: three to six a year under EUR 50m at 5-7x EBITDA, roughly SEK 300-800m a year; at those multiples post-tax returns are about 11-15% before synergies; liquidity SEK 2,090m and 1.3x leverage support it.
  • Working capital: growth absorbs about 23-26% of incremental sales.
  • Implication: mid-term growth of about 10-14% a year including acquisitions, return on new capital in the low-to-mid teens.
C-51

How high are the returns on capital: post-tax return on new invested capital, on capital employed and on tangible capital?

❌ D45

Judgement. Returns on capital are mediocre and recovering: post-tax return on invested capital is about 10% on 2026 adjusted earnings and about 11-12% on the second-half run-rate, roughly at or slightly above the cost of capital; return on new invested capital in acquisitions is about 11-15%; return on tangible capital is higher (about 16-20% post-tax, an inference because goodwill and customer relationships dominate the balance sheet). None meets the framework's high-return thresholds.

  • Invested capital (Q3 2026, approximate): equity SEK 4,896m plus net debt excluding leases SEK 1,305m plus lease liabilities about SEK 0.5bn gives about SEK 6.7bn.
  • Returns: FY2026 adjusted EBITA about SEK 870m, taxed at 25%, gives about SEK 650m, about 10% of invested capital; at the H2 2026 run-rate about 11%; at 2027 levels about 11-12% as capital also grows with acquisitions and working capital. Business-foundations research cites ROIC of about 11%; return on equity has been below 20% for five years; ROIC peaked near 40% in 2013; asset turnover fell from 3.41x (2011) to below 1.5x (2025).
  • Tangible capital: net working capital SEK 2,072m (Q2 2026) plus plants; the 2021-24 capacity build (about SEK 1.5bn of capex) lowered tangible returns, and some European capacity remains under-used.
C-52

How high is the conversion of net income into free cash flow, and how do net working capital and similar items behave?

❌ D45

Judgement. Cash conversion is volatile and structurally held down by working capital: five-year operating cash flow conversion has been below 80% of net income, net working capital runs at about 23-26% of sales, and growth absorbs cash in proportion. 2026 conversion should be reasonable (Q4 is the seasonal release quarter), but in growth years free cash flow after working capital should be modelled at about 60-90% of net income before acquisitions.

  • History (FactSet): operating cash flow SEK 944m (2023), 921m (2024), 585m (2025); capex 510m, 316m, 94m; FactSet free cash flow 414m, 605m, 491m. Company cash conversion 235% (Q4 2025), 26% (Q1 2026), 84% (Q2), 72% (Q3).
  • 2026: nine-month operating cash flow SEK 438m, capex SEK 91m, working capital use SEK 205m (against SEK 215m a year earlier); Q4 typically releases cash (Q4 2025 freed SEK 144m from inventory). FactSet's FY2026 free-cash-flow consensus of SEK 70m appears to reflect acquisitions as well; on operating cash flow less capex, FY2026 is likely about SEK 600-700m.
  • Working capital behaviour: net working capital SEK 2,072m (26.1% of trailing sales) at Q2 2026; DSO 56-60 days with longer municipal terms; inventory SEK 1,202m with a SEK 212m obsolescence reserve at end-2025 (a key audit matter); supply constraints now push inventory up.
  • Model rule: each SEK 1bn of extra revenue ties up about SEK 230-260m of working capital.
C-53

How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?

C58

Judgement. Margins are moderate and rising from the trough, with a ceiling below the 2022-23 peak. Group adjusted EBITA margin went from 7.2% (Q4 2025) to 11.3% (Q3 2026); a realistic path is about 10.0-10.3% for 2026, 10.5-11.5% for 2027 and 11-12% for 2028. The ceiling is set by the services mix (lower gross margin, decent EBITA margin), European duct pricing and the glass cost squeeze.

  • Gross margin (adjusted): 40.5% (Q4 2023), 41.4% (Q4 2024), 40.1% (Q2 2025), 37.5% (Q3 2025), 37.5% (Q4 2025), 38.7% (Q2 2026), 39.8% (Q3 2026). (The FactSet gross-profit line is on an unexplained basis; do not use it.)
  • Operating margin (adjusted EBITA): 16.6% (2022), 15.1% (2023), 10.6% (2024), 8.4% (2025); 8.6%, 10.0%, 11.3% in Q1-Q3 2026. Adjusted operating costs 25.0% of sales in Q3 2026 (26.2% a year earlier).
  • Segments: Fiber Solutions 6.4% (2025), 5.2% (Q4 2025), 9.1% (Q2), 9.4% (Q3 2026), target 10% through the cycle; Data Center 17.9% (2025), 16.8%, 15.5%, 16.9% (Q1-Q3 2026), range 15-17%, target 15%; Harsh Environment 11.2% (2025), 10.0% (Q2), 11.0% (Q3), target 15%.
  • Peers: consensus EBITDA margins 13-14% for Hexatronic against Prysmian 13-14%, NKT 11-16%, Nokia 15-18%; Corning's optical margins are structurally higher (20%-plus).
C-54

How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode earnings per share?

❌ D48

Judgement. The earnings base is cleaner in 2026 than in 2025 but still noisy: the gap between adjusted and reported earnings was the whole of FY2025's earnings, Q3 2026's reported EBITA was flattered by a SEK 67m impairment reversal, and several items between adjusted EBITA and EPS (acquisition amortisation, contingent-consideration revaluations, tax-asset assessments, capitalised development) can move EPS by 5-15% in a quarter. The model must state its EPS basis every time.

  • FY2025: reported EPS SEK -0.07 against FactSet adjusted SEK 0.80, the Viewforth definition SEK 1.17 and brokers' basis SEK -0.33; SEK 298m of non-recurring costs (restructuring, facility, transition and legal); rolling twelve-month EBITA carried SEK 134m of non-recurring items against an adjusted SEK 889m.
  • 2026: Q3 reported EBITA SEK 352m against adjusted SEK 281m (+SEK 71m net one-offs, including the reversal); Q3 net financial items -SEK 73m, including SEK 26m net interest and SEK 28m of revaluations of contingent consideration and acquisition options.
  • Recurring leakages: amortisation of acquired intangibles about SEK 25m a quarter (about SEK 100-110m a year, rising with each deal); earn-outs (JOWO up to EUR 7.6m, Superior up to USD 3m) whose revaluations hit financial items as acquisitions outperform; capitalised development costs; intangibles amortised over 3-10 years.
  • Tax: Q3 2026 29.6%, nine months 26.7% (helped by deferred-tax-asset assessments), FY2024 31.3%; a normal rate of about 25-27% is the prudent model assumption, above the 24% many models use.
  • Dilution: 15.8m new shares (May 2026); incentive dilution about 2.2% including existing rights.
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

C58

Judgement. Fixed-cost operating leverage is material and two-way, and it is the single most important mechanism in the next two years' numbers. The 2023-25 downturn showed decremental margins close to 100% on lost sales at group level; the 2026 recovery shows incremental margins of about 30% on organic volume at group level and nearly 40% in Fiber Solutions, on a cost base cut by SEK 120m. A 10% swing in Fiber Solutions organic volume moves group adjusted EBITA by roughly SEK 150-200m, about 15-20% of 2027 EBITA, which makes the cone of outcomes wide.

  • Down-cycle evidence: revenue SEK 8,150m (2023) to 7,519m (2025), -8%, while adjusted EBITA fell from SEK 1,234m to 632m (-49%): about SEK 600m of profit lost on about SEK 630m of revenue lost (price, currency and mix included). Fiber Solutions margin fell to 5.2% in Q4 2025 on European under-absorption.
  • Up-cycle evidence: Q3 2026 against Q3 2025: sales +SEK 615m, adjusted EBITA +SEK 135m (22% overall; about 30% on organic volume after stripping about SEK 264m of acquired sales at about 12% margins); Fiber Solutions sales about +SEK 174m and EBITA +SEK 66m (about 38%); gross margin +230bp and operating-cost ratio -120bp.
  • Rules of thumb for the model: Fiber Solutions incremental and decremental margins about 30-40% on organic volume; Data Center about 15-20% (installation labour is largely variable); Harsh Environment about 25-30% (plant-based). Clinton capacity retained means fixed costs are back in the base, raising leverage both ways.
  • Range: with Fiber Solutions about SEK 5bn in 2027, ±10% volume is about ±SEK 500m of sales and ±SEK 150-200m of EBITA.
C-76

How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?

C70

Judgement. Financial leverage is low and comfortable after the May 2026 equity raise and strong 2026 cash flow, so it amplifies neither EPS nor risk much: adjusted leverage is 1.3x, net interest is about 10% of adjusted EBITA, and covenant headroom is ample. The risk is not the current gearing but the policy: leverage is intended to be re-used for acquisitions, and directed equity issues are the stated funding route for larger deals.

  • Debt: net debt excluding leases SEK 1,305m (Q3 2026) against SEK 1,470m (Q2) and SEK 1,582m (end-2025); FactSet's lease-inclusive net debt was SEK 1,946m at Q2 (1.9x its EBITDA basis). Borrowing deliberately held in USD (88m), EUR (8m) and GBP (26m) as a net-investment hedge.
  • Cost: net interest SEK 81m for nine months (SEK 26m in Q3); net financial items are noisier (Q3 -SEK 73m) because of earn-out revaluations.
  • Capacity: available liquidity SEK 2,090m (cash SEK 678m plus SEK 1,412m unused facilities); at 2.0x leverage roughly SEK 0.8-1.0bn more could be borrowed for acquisitions.
  • EPS effect: falling interest adds roughly 1-3% a year to EPS through 2028 absent acquisitions.
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

❌ D45

Judgement. The easy cost cuts have been made. Raw materials (about 60% of sales) are pass-through, not cuttable; data-centre labour is variable with activity; what remains cuttable is a further consolidation of European duct capacity (perhaps SEK 50-100m, an inference), procurement, and some overhead in a decentralised structure that resists centralisation. Large cuts would require closing plants that Europe's connection market and the US upswing may still need.

  • Already cut: SEK 120m a year (Weterings closure, 120 FTEs, Clinton downsizing, European consolidation), completed by Q1 2026; the Clinton element has been reversed.
  • Cost structure (approximate): raw materials about 60% of sales (SEK 1,377m on SEK 2,245m in Q2 2026); adjusted operating costs about 25% of sales; plant fixed costs and depreciation (FactSet D&A about SEK 400m a year including leases and amortisation).
  • Cuttable: European duct footprint (Fiber Solutions Europe still falling 9% in Q3); purchasing (new supplier validation); overhead in acquired entities (limited by the decentralised model).
  • Not cuttable without damage: US plants (demand rising); training, certification and field support (the moat); data-centre technicians (scarce).
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C62

Judgement. Beyond cost, there is plausible and congruent scope to lift operating profit by roughly SEK 250-350m over the mid term from a few big areas: Harsh Environment margin recovery toward 15% (about SEK 60-80m), Fiber Solutions moving through 10% on US volume (about SEK 50-100m), the 2028 subsea line (about SEK 60-65m on Redeye's estimate), and continued mix shift and acquired-margin improvement; working capital is a cash lever of about SEK 300-400m. Peer precedents show such improvements are achievable in this industry when capacity is scarce, but they also show they reverse when it is not.

  • Few big areas: Harsh Environment (SEK 1.4bn of sales, margin 11% against 15% target; dynamic cables recovery and JOWO); Fiber Solutions absorption (target 10% through the cycle; Q3 9.4%); subsea capacity from about SEK 150-200m to above SEK 500m by 2028 under the NKT framework; working capital from about 26% toward 22% of sales.
  • Many small areas: pricing on scarce products, four-region simplification, services cross-sell, acquired companies' margins toward 15%.
  • Peer precedents: Prysmian's Digital Solutions margin from 16.8% to 23.8% (Q2 2025 to Q2 2026) on repricing and capacity reallocation to hyperscalers; Belden to 16.9% on a solutions mix; NKT's transmission margin from 15% to 20% on contract vintages.
  • Congruence: every lever is one management has stated.
C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

❌ D45

Judgement. The business perimeter is moderate and widening, which lowers predictability: three segments across 18 plants, at least fifteen countries, a decentralised structure of independent acquired entities, and a policy of adding three to six units a year. Geographic concentration has shifted from Europe to North America (48% of Q3 sales), which reduces dependence on European FTTH but increases exposure to the US cycle and to the krona-dollar rate.

  • Geography: 2025 USA 32.8%, Germany 15.4%, UK 12.2%, Sweden 8.7%, other 30.9%; Q3 2026 North America 48%, Europe 43%, Asia-Pacific 9%.
  • Units: three segments; four Fiber Solutions regions; acquired entities kept separate; lumpy project drivers (subsea shipments, data-centre projects, oil-and-gas timing).
  • Predictability effect: segment reporting makes the drivers countable; the number of things that can go wrong grows with each deal; FX translation and acquisition consolidation blur organic trends.

Company - Management

C5811 questions
C-5568C-5664C-5768C-57b45C-5855C-5955C-6058C-6160C-6252C-6355C-6452

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Management under Rikard Fröberg has earned credibility by acting: a cost programme delivered above plan and early, a segment structure that makes the business trackable, three cheap acquisitions that are performing, equity raised near the highs, a subsea partnership, and four quarters of results at or above consensus on profit. The ambition is large and the strategy dynamic, with three demand cycles that do not move together. The weaknesses are depth and scaffolding: the leadership team has thinned at the growth engine and in finance, the governance is minimum-compliant (no internal audit, thin director holdings, a discounted directed issue), communication withholds the transmission mechanism (no guidance, no orders, no price split), and the company's mid-term record before 2025 was poor. For the model, management's record argues for giving weight to stated near-term cues (they have proved conservative in 2026) and for crediting continued acquisition cadence at stated multiples, but against crediting the 12% 2028 margin target in full; the board's own incentive range (9.8-11.1% average margin over 2026-28) is the better anchor.

C-55

How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

C68

Judgement. Ambition is big relative to Hexatronic's size and is a genuine force: management targets SEK 11bn of sales by 2028 (from SEK 7.5bn in 2025, about 13% a year including acquisitions), about 12% EBITA, Data Center at SEK 3bn, three to six acquisitions a year and a doubling of subsea capacity, and it reversed a capacity cut to capture US share. Against peers, the ambition is bolder than Ericsson's and Nexans' and comparable in intent to Prysmian's and NKT's capacity bets, but smaller in absolute scale; the headline margin ambition is not matched by the incentive plan, whose 9.8-11.1% average-margin range suggests the board's working expectation is lower.

  • Growth as a priority: Data Center from about SEK 1.4bn (2025) to SEK 3bn (2028); acquisitions explicitly part of the target; US multi-hub build; services broadened.
  • Outcompeting and adapting: Clinton reversal; transport-network products; training centres for alt-nets.
  • Restraint: targets not raised after three beats; the chief executive says the company is "not resting on any laurels"; group-level targets were scrapped in 2025 in favour of segment targets, then a group figure reappeared in May 2026.
C-56

How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

C64

Judgement. The strategy is smart and dynamic in its main choices: it pivots away from the commodity FTTH duct cycle toward data-centre and defence niches with certification and service lock-in, cuts European capacity while localising in the US, and uses a partner framework (NKT) rather than speculative capacity for subsea. Its weaknesses are that the moat it widens runs through services and certifications rather than the scarce asset (glass), that part of the growth is bought rather than built, and that it carries no explicit return hurdles.

  • Right and bold priorities: pivot chosen when the FTTH cycle broke; the European response (cost-out, connection products) fits a shrinking market; the US response (localisation, direct channel, capacity retained) fits a growing one; subsea expansion underwritten by a customer framework to 2032.
  • Moat widening: through services (day-2 teams), certifications (defence) and BABA compliance; not through upstream integration, which a company of this size cannot fund.
  • Dynamic across environments: the portfolio now has three cycles (US fibre, data centre, defence/energy) that do not move together; in a hyperscaler pause, US Fiber Solutions and defence still carry earnings; in a US slowdown, Data Center still grows.
  • Gaps: services adjacency into security and audio-visual is further from the core; no acquisition return threshold; the South Carolina cable-line deferral to 2027 delays BABA-compliant cable while BEAD contracts are being let.
C-57

How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

C68

Judgement. Judged by actions, the chief executive has been an enabler: in eighteen months he launched and over-delivered a cost programme, restructured the organisation into accountable segments, made three cheap acquisitions that are performing, raised equity near the highs, signed a strategic subsea partnership and reversed a capacity cut when demand turned, and the results followed. The record is short, he has no fibre or listed-company chief-executive background, and his framing of results leans promotional, but the actions outweigh the conjecture.

  • Actions: performance programme launched four months into the role (Q3 2025); SEK 120m of savings against SEK 110m, ahead of schedule; Weterings closed; three acquisitions at 4.7-6.0x EBITDA; SEK 600m raised at SEK 38 in May 2026 (the share is SEK 40.94 on 22 October); voluntary segment reporting; NKT partnership; four consecutive quarters at or above consensus on profit (Q4 2025 to Q3 2026).
  • Against: no fibre or public-company chief-executive experience (uncited source); presented a timing-assisted Q3 as "clear validation"; withheld price magnitudes; the deputy chief executive left mid-pivot; he departed Ansell without a fixed term, forfeiting incentives (a career-administrator flag in the leadership research).
  • Alignment: bought 45,000 savings shares with his own money under LTIP 2025; holds 189,000 performance share rights; total 2025 pay SEK 9.4m, below his predecessor's peak.
C-57b

How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

❌ D45

Judgement. Hiring well is visible as a priority in redeployment (moving the CFO who designed the turnaround into the largest segment, promoting internal M&A talent into Data Center), but the leadership team is thinner than it was a year ago and two key seats (permanent CFO, permanent Data Center head) are open; the record of building a great team is not yet established.

  • Strengths: CFO Lindén (since 2021) moving to Fiber Solutions; Jakob Skov (Harsh Environment) retained; Oscar Wärme internal; Patrik Johannesson as Head of IR since January 2026; Steve Ramel appointed UK chief executive; no senior external hire into the executive team is evidenced since the chief executive himself.
  • Weaknesses: deputy chief executive left; Head of IR turnover (one year); Priess out; executive team reduced from eight to seven; no named CFO successor or timetable; no succession plans disclosed for CEO or CFO.
  • Test: quality and speed of the CFO and Data Center appointments by February 2027.
C-58

How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

C55

Judgement. The business is well resourced financially and adequately resourced physically, but its human and upstream resources are stretched: liquidity and plant capacity are buffers, while management bandwidth, technicians for services, and secured glass supply are not. It is a moderately well-built ant colony, not one built for a multi-year shortage.

  • Financial buffer: liquidity SEK 2,090m; adjusted leverage 1.3x; equity SEK 4,896m.
  • Physical: 18 plants; four US plants including Ogden (completed in 2024 for about USD 30m of equipment); Clinton retained; subsea line under construction for 2028; capex restrained at about 1.5% of sales (a risk of under-investment in a capacity-short market).
  • People: 2,133 employees (growth mainly acquired); 2025 turnover 17% (349 departures); management stretched; technicians scarce.
  • Supply: no upstream integration; validating new glass and connector suppliers only now.
C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

C55

Judgement. The culture is coherent in its own terms (decentralised "freedom with responsibility", acquired founders retained, decisions close to customers) and incentives are reasonably aligned with shareholders, but there is no evidence of the whole-system congruence seen at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair, and the culture was forged in a growth decade and has been tested only briefly by central cost-cutting and leadership churn.

  • Supportive evidence: employee satisfaction index 74%; sick leave 3.1%; eNPS above target in 2024; 42 executives invested their own money in 149,061 savings shares; LTIP weights EPS 40%, sales growth 25%, EBITA margin 25%, sustainability 10% over a three-year average.
  • Tensions: 120 job cuts plus 45 at Hudiksvall; 17% staff turnover in 2025; decentralisation limits group-wide synergies and may mask weak central integration controls (no internal audit function); the CEO's short-term incentive cap raised to 80% of salary.
  • Congruence verdict: goals, strategy and incentives align; execution systems (working capital, central controls, capacity planning) are less congruent with the stated ambition.
C-60

How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

C58

Judgement. Communication is clear on structure and helpful on direction, honest on near-term caution, and weak on the transmission mechanism: the company reports segments voluntarily, commissions a consensus, and warned explicitly that Q4 Data Center will normalise, but it gives no numeric guidance, no order intake or book-to-bill, no price-versus-volume split, and withholds the size of price increases. A modeller can build the year only from segment data and qualitative cues.

  • Helpful: three segments with organic growth, margin and geography each quarter; Modular Finance consensus published before results; explicit Q4 2026 cues (Data Center to first-half pattern, Q4 and Q1 seasonally lower, submarine similar to Q3, US growth continuing, Europe subdued); order book duration (about four months); Redeye calls communication "transparent and timely".
  • Unhelpful: no annual guidance; no order intake, backlog ageing or cancellation data; magnitudes of raw-material price increases withheld; BEAD timing hedged; FTTH metric moved from penetration to homes passed; cash conversion and financial-item noise placed less prominently than record sales.
C-61

How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

C60

Judgement. The recent record of acting is strong and the mid-term record is poor, so the company's delivery credentials rest on eighteen months under the new chief executive. The current team has delivered in a mixed environment (record sales and margin recovery with Europe still falling and supply constraining), but the company's 2022-25 history is one of a boom over-built, a bust under-anticipated, profit warnings and repeated commissioning slips.

  • Recent: restructuring delivered ahead of plan; three acquisitions closed and performing; equity raised; Q4 2025, Q2 2026 and Q3 2026 beat on sales and profit; Q1 2026 missed on sales (weather) but beat on profit.
  • Mid-term: adjusted EBITA margin 16.6% (2022) to 8.4% (2025); consensus FY2026 EPS cut from SEK 5.05 to SEK 1.76; Q2 2025 profit warning; Redeye base case cut from SEK 57 to SEK 24 during 2025; capacity commissioning slips at Clinton (2022), South Carolina (2023), Ogden (2024) and the cable-line move (to 2027).
  • Outperformance in a tough environment: Q3 2026 organic growth of 17% while European Fiber Solutions fell 9% and supply bound.
C-62

To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

C52

Judgement. Hexatronic shows outlier standards in parts of the business (US growth well above peers, installation speed, training) but not across the system: European factories were left under-absorbed for several quarters before action, Harsh Environment sits 400-500bp below target, working capital is not tight, and there is no record of out-executing the integrated majors in data centres.

  • Outlier evidence: North America +80% in Q3 2026 against Dura-Line +23% (Q1 2026); Fiber Solutions organic +13% against Prysmian's 9.4% group organic growth in Q2; expert calls on lead times and training centres; cost programme over-delivered.
  • Non-outlier evidence: Fiber Solutions margin fell to 5.2% before the programme; Harsh Environment margin 10-11% against 15%; net working capital about 26% of sales; DSO about 60 days against Clearfield's 40-44.
C-63

Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

C55

Judgement. The evidence shows governance that is sound on paper and shareholder-friendly in incentive design, but thin in stewardship and scaffolding, and capital decisions that put growth ahead of minority holders' preferences (a directed issue at a discount without a rights offer; no dividend while acquiring). Shareholders are not placed behind management, but the scaffolding is minimum-compliant rather than prudent beyond what was needed.

  • Shareholder-first evidence: six independent directors out of six; independent audit and remuneration committees; clean 2025 AGM discharge; no related-party transactions; clawback rights; LTIP on a three-year average after holder consultation; management invests its own money in savings shares; board and management own about 13% (management about 6%); CEO pay modest.
  • Against: no internal audit function; PwC sole auditor since 2013; four directors hold fewer than 20,000 shares each; the chair's 200,000 call options were issued by Accendo, a large holder; CEO short-term incentive cap raised to 80% of salary; SEK 600m directed issue at an 8.2% discount to institutions without a rights offer; no anchor owner above 10% of votes.
  • Why: a fragmented register and an acquisition-led strategy make speed and institutional funding more valuable to management than pre-emption; the board was refreshed in 2024 and has not yet built long-tenure stewardship.
C-64

To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

C52

Judgement. The current team allocates capital sensibly as an acquirer (small deals at 4.7-6.4x EBITDA with earn-outs, equity raised near the high rather than debt at 2.2x leverage, subsea capacity backed by a customer framework), but Hexatronic is not a dynamic repurchaser and its longer record includes a costly 2021-23 capacity build at the top of the FTTH cycle that left plants under-used and returns depressed. The allocation record is improving, not yet enlightened.

  • Good decisions: acquisition multiples; SEK 600m equity at SEK 38 in May 2026; postponing the South Carolina cable line to protect cash in 2025; retaining Clinton when demand turned; NKT framework before committing subsea capex.
  • Weak decisions (legacy): heavy 2021-23 capex (SEK 462-510m a year) into US and European duct capacity at the peak; acquisitions over 70% of Data Center's historical growth; return on invested capital fell from about 40% (2013) to about 11%.
  • No buybacks or dividends: stated policy acquisitions first.

Risks

❌ D404 questions
C-8075C-8142C-8230C-8330

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The fundamental risk profile is moderate, with the growth engine accelerating, estimates rising and operating flags absent, but the risks that do exist are concentrated in two correlated demand channels (US carrier spending and hyperscaler spending) and amplified by high operating leverage, so a single adverse turn would move earnings by 15-25%. On top sits a large perception and mark-to-market risk that is independent of fundamentals: a small, thinly covered, illiquid stock that has more than doubled, tied to an AI theme whose financing has cracked, in a market regime that penalises exactly this profile, with a trough-multiple downside of about 25-40% on intact earnings and 50-60% on trough earnings. For the second-set Analyst, the risk work argues for an explicit downside scenario (US refill ending plus Data Center normalisation: 2027 EPS about SEK 2.0-2.3) alongside the base case, for modelling a stronger krona as a live sensitivity, and for treating Q4 2026 Data Center sales and H1 2027 North American growth as the two observable tests.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B75

Judgement. The core growth engine has not decelerated over the last twelve months; it has accelerated sharply and is running well above the company's normal organic rate. The forward risk is that the rate of change has peaked: Data Center normalises in Q4 2026, the US comparisons harden from Q2 2027, and supply constraints cap volume, so a deceleration of the second derivative is likely in H1 2027 even if the input force (data-centre build, US fibre rebuild) does not itself slow.

  • Revenue growth against normal: group organic +3% (2025), -2% (Q1 2026), +11% (Q2), +17% (Q3); reported growth +18% (Q2) and +33% (Q3). The company's long-run organic norm outside the 2021-22 boom is roughly mid-to-high single digits.
  • Input force: Data Center organic 39% (Q3 2025), 20% (Q1 2026), 27% (Q2), 44% (Q3, timing-assisted); North American Fiber Solutions +44% (Q2); North America +80% (Q3); hyperscaler capex still rising; BEAD construction starting. No sign of the input force slowing in the files.
  • The one slowing line: Harsh Environment organic -4% (Q2) and +6% (Q3), on oil-and-gas deferrals; not the growth engine.
  • Watch: Q4 2026 Data Center sales against the first-half average (about SEK 480m a quarter); North American growth in Q2-Q3 2027 against hard comparisons.
C-81

What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

❌ D42

Judgement. The case carries several sudden, high-magnitude, low-to-moderate-probability fundamental risks, and they are correlated through the same two channels (US carrier spending and hyperscaler spending) and amplified by operating leverage. The most plausible and largest are a US refill ending and a financing-led hyperscaler pause; the most idiosyncratic is a glass or connector delivery failure.

  • US refill ends abruptly (probability over 18 months about 20-25%; magnitude large): North American Fiber Solutions volume -20-30% would cut group adjusted EBITA by about 15-25% through 30-40% decremental margins; Clinton capacity just retained becomes under-absorbed again; early warning: North American growth falling into single digits before comparisons harden.
  • Hyperscaler capex pause or AI financing event (about 15-20%; large): Data Center organic growth turning negative and margins compressing toward 12-13%; group EBITA -10-20%; the CoreWeave bond fall shows the financing channel is live.
  • Glass or connector delivery failure or allocation cut (about 10-15%; medium-large): a quarter of missed shipments, customer allocation decisions and inventory build; non-integrated cable makers have failed on guaranteed deliveries elsewhere.
  • Acquisition or integration failure and goodwill impairment (about 10%; medium): goodwill and customer relationships dominate the balance sheet; a reported loss and credibility hit; the M&A architect has left.
  • Finance-control event during the CFO transition (about 5%; medium): errors in contingent consideration, inventory valuation (a key audit matter; SEK 212m reserve) or project accounting.
  • Currency shock (about 25-30% for a 10% stronger krona; medium): about -9% reported revenue, smaller EBITA effect.
  • Hudiksvall permit refusal or delay (about 10%; medium for 2028): subsea step delayed.
  • Policy reversal (BABA, tariffs) (about 10%; medium): imports re-enter the US duct market.
C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

❌ D30

Judgement. Perception risk is high and is the most likely source of a large share-price fall unrelated to fundamentals. Hexatronic is a small-capitalisation Swedish stock with about USD 7m of daily trading, no anchor owner, one visible broker, a twelve-month gain of 113%, and a narrative tied to AI optics at the moment the AI theme's financing has cracked and the regime has become a discount-rate shock. A 20-35% drawdown with fundamentals intact is plausible; the shares fell 18.7% from the July-August high with no company news.

  • Theme read-across: optics and fibre cohort -4.5% over three months on "AI monetization anxiety"; a hyperscaler capex cut, a Corning or Prysmian disappointment (both report Q3 in late October to November), or another AI credit event would hit every optical name.
  • Regime: US 10-year about 5.1%; Fragility rated Double Underweight (small, levered or illiquid names penalised); small-cap growth cracks before large caps; Swedish listings exposed to a likely November Riksbank hike.
  • Stock-specific: thin coverage (one broker named; four to five in Modular Finance); insider selling possible after the May lock-up and the deputy chief executive's notice period; an extended trend (Stage 2 "proven") that has banked much of its upside.
  • Narrative fragility: the bear case (Redeye bear SEK 16, the risk report's "false friend") lives in qualitative framing and is not modelled by the covering broker; if a broker quantified it, positioning could rebalance sharply.
C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

❌ D30

Judgement. On intact earnings, a fall to a fair trough multiple would take the shares about 25-40% lower; on trough earnings at a trough multiple, about 50-60% lower. A fair trough multiple for a mid-quality, no-dividend cyclical with a recent bust is about 9-10x 24-month-forward earnings (the stock's two-year median is about 10x; its ten-year 10th percentile is 8.2x; it touched 5.2x in 2024-25).

  • Starting point: SEK 40.94 (22 October 2026) on 24-month-forward consensus EPS of about SEK 2.96 (dashboard), a forward P/E of about 13.4-13.8x (52nd percentile of ten years; 80th of two years; ten-year bands: median 13.6x, 35th 11.7x, 10th 8.2x).
  • Intact earnings: 10x gives SEK 29.6 (-28%); 8.2x gives SEK 24.3 (-41%).
  • Trough earnings: if the US refill ends or hyperscalers pause, 24-month-forward EPS of about SEK 2.2-2.4; at 8-9x, SEK 18-21 (-49% to -56%).
  • Extreme analogue: the 2024-25 multiple of 5-6x on about SEK 2.0 gives SEK 10-12; Redeye's bear case is SEK 16 (5% terminal EBIT margin, 11% WACC).
  • Probability-weighted trough: about SEK 24-26 (roughly -40%).

Guidance

C566 questions
C-6855C-7058C-7160C-7250C-8562C-7350

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Hexatronic does not guide numerically; it sets destination targets for 2028 that include acquisitions and gives qualitative quarterly cues, and since the 2025 rebasing those cues have been conservative (the Q3 margin warning did not materialise; the Q4 Data Center normalisation is pre-announced). The 2028 targets are moderately conservative in Data Center, about right in Fiber Solutions and stretched in Harsh Environment, and the board's incentive range (9.8-11.1% average margin over 2026-28) is a more realistic anchor than the 12% headline. For the model: treat the Q4 cues as a slight under-statement; set FY2026 at about SEK 8.55-8.7bn of sales and SEK 860-890m of adjusted EBITA; do not take the SEK 11bn target as an organic forecast (strip out roughly SEK 0.8-1.2bn of future acquired revenue); and expect estimates to be driven by results surprises rather than by guidance changes, given that coverage is effectively one broker.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C55

Judgement. There is no numeric company guidance for this year or next, so the comparison with consensus runs through the qualitative cues and the 2028 targets. The Q4 2026 cues imply a full year of about SEK 8.55-8.7bn of sales and SEK 860-890m of adjusted EBITA, above the pre-Q3 covering-broker model (SEK 784-820m of EBITA) and roughly in line with or slightly above FactSet's median revenue (SEK 8,512m), so FY2026 consensus profit should rise by about 5-10% after Q3. For 2027 the company gives nothing numeric. The 2028 targets (SEK 11bn; about 12% EBITA) sit about 10% above consensus revenue (SEK 10.0bn) and modestly above consensus margin (Redeye about 11.7%), mainly because the targets include future acquisitions and the broker's model excludes them.

  • Q4 2026 cues (22 October 2026): Data Center to normalise toward the first-half pattern after favourable seasonality and project timing in Q3; Q4 and Q1 seasonally lower than Q2 and Q3; submarine deliveries similar to Q3; continued US growth; European FTTH and transport subdued; glass and connectors constraining growth.
  • Why the gap: management avoids near-term numbers after the 2023-25 misses, sets destination targets that include acquisitions, and uses qualitative caution (the Q3 margin warning in July did not materialise); the broker models only what is organic and visible.
  • Implication: expect 2026 estimates to move up modestly and 2027 estimates to move little until the Q4 report shows the size of Data Center normalisation.
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C58

Judgement. Because the company gives no numeric guidance, it has no record of upgrading guidance in the usual sense; its behaviour is better read as a shift from an over-promising pattern before 2025 to an honest-tracker, cautious pattern since: targets were rebased down in September 2025, near-term cautions have since proved conservative, and the 2028 targets have been held rather than raised despite three beats. The chief executive under-promises near term and frames results confidently after the event.

  • Before 2025 (prior chief executive): a "temporary blip" narrative for FTTH; BEAD expectations pushed from H2 2025 to H2 2026; misses in Q1 2024, Q4 2024, Q1 2025 and Q2 2025 (profit warning).
  • Rebasing (September 2025): long-term FTTH outlook softened; group targets replaced by segment targets; the FTTH metric moved to homes passed; restructuring costs taken.
  • Since: a July 2026 warning of Q3 margin pressure followed by a margin of 11.3%; Q4 normalisation flagged in advance; targets "on track" but not raised.
  • Read-across: the sandbagging pattern seen at Prysmian and NKT (low initial ranges, raised in-year) cannot apply directly without numeric guidance, but the qualitative cues have the same conservative bias.
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

C60

Judgement. Adjusted for the stronger change forces now acting on the business, the current long-term guidance is moderately conservative in Data Center, about right in Fiber Solutions, and stretched in Harsh Environment; the group 12% margin is realistic only with Harsh Environment reaching 15%, and the board's own incentive range points to about 11% on average over 2026-28. The near-term qualitative guidance is conservative.

  • Against the past: revenue grew about 45% a year in 2019-23 (boom plus acquisitions) and fell about 4% a year in 2023-25; margins fell from 16.6% to 8.4%. The 2028 target implies about 13% a year from 2026 including acquisitions, with margins recovering to about 12%.
  • Change forces now versus then: stronger than 2024-25 (data centres, US rebuild, defence) but narrower than the 2021-22 FTTH boom, and capped by supply.
  • Segment check: Data Center about SEK 2.2bn in 2026 (Q3 alone SEK 692m) to SEK 3bn in 2028 is about 17% a year, below its recent organic pace plus acquisitions: conservative. Fiber Solutions about SEK 4.95bn to SEK 6bn at 10% is about 10% a year, achievable only with sustained US growth and a stable Europe: about right to moderately demanding. Harsh Environment about SEK 1.4bn to SEK 2bn at 15% (from 11%) needs acquisitions and a dynamic-cable recovery: stretched.
  • Incentive anchor: LTIP 2026 pays on an average EBITA margin of 9.8-11.1% over 2026-28.
C-72

How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

C50

Judgement. Guidance is specific at the destination and vague on the path: the 2028 targets are built from segment building blocks (sales and margin per segment), but there is no annual guidance, no organic versus acquired split, no acquisition budget or return threshold, and no capex or cash guidance other than the subsea project; the quarterly qualitative cues are useful and specific in direction.

  • Specific: segment targets (Fiber Solutions SEK 6bn at 10%; Harsh Environment SEK 2bn at 15%; Data Center SEK 3bn at 15%); acquisition framework (three to six a year, under EUR 50m, 5-7x EBITDA); capex guideline of 3-4% of sales (2025 call), actually running at 1.5-3%.
  • Vague: how much of the SEK 11bn is acquired; when Harsh Environment margins recover; what the subsea line costs; how fast pass-through works; what cash conversion to expect.
C-85

To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C62

Judgement. Informal commentary is above formal guidance (which is absent) and modestly above pre-Q3 consensus for 2026 and 2027: management says it sees no slowdown in underlying Data Center momentum, expects continued US growth and market-share opportunities, highlights transport-network demand and reversed a capacity cut, while the covering broker's preview assumed a sharp deceleration in 2027. The commentary is tempered by explicit cautions (Q4 normalisation, supply constraints, Europe), which management uses to avoid explicit commitments after 2023-25.

  • Above: "clear validation" of the strategy; Data Center market strong, cloud to outgrow enterprise, services to expand organically and by acquisition; US growth continuing; Clinton downsizing reversed; strong submarine order book; defence demand strong for years.
  • Below or neutral: Q4 Data Center normalisation; seasonal Q4/Q1; glass and connector constraints; Europe subdued; oil and gas muted.
  • Consensus reference: FactSet median revenue growth for 2027 is +9.8%; the Q3 review reports the covering broker's preview modelling about 4% for 2027 (other figures in the same review imply more, so treat the broker's 2027 organic assumption as low).
C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C50

Judgement. Three features of the guidance and investor relations are uncommon and material: the company commissions and publishes its own consensus (four to five contributors) before each print, which helps calibrate surprises but shows how thin coverage is; its 2028 targets explicitly include acquisitions, which makes them non-comparable with broker models; and its incentive plan's margin range sits below its headline target. Coverage breadth is the most material constraint: FactSet shows one broker by name, so consensus can move on one model.

  • IR practice: Modular Finance consensus; quarterly webcasts; Head of IR changed twice in two years; Deutsche Bank and others cover the sector but not the stock in the files.
  • Disclosure gaps that matter for the model: no order intake, no Fiber Solutions order book, no volume and price split, no project-level submarine timing beyond quarterly cues.

Sell-side estimates

C623 questions
C-6762C-6965C-7460

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell-side picture is thin (one visible broker, four to five in the company's own consensus) but coherent: estimates were cut by about two-thirds in 2023-25, the base was rebased in September 2025, and the company has beaten in four of the last five quarters, with Q3 2026 the largest beat (EBITA +21%, EPS +37% against the four-contributor consensus). The 2026-28 consensus path is credible and slightly conservative on Data Center revenue and on Fiber Solutions margin, so modest upward revisions are likely after Q3 for 2026 and less so for 2027; the revision momentum had paused before the print (one-month and three-month changes near zero) and will be set by whether Q4 2026 Data Center normalisation is mild. The consensus does not carry the downside scenarios (US refill ending, stronger krona, supply caps), and the 2029 figure rests on one broker. For the model, use consensus 2027-28 as a reasonable central anchor with mild upside on revenue and margin, and build explicit downside cases rather than leaning on the sell-side's.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

C62

Judgement. The sell-side's forecasts (effectively one model, pre-Q3) project a smooth recovery to a growth and margin path well above 2024-25 and below the 2022-23 peak: revenue +13% (2026), +10% (2027), +7% (2028); brokers'-basis EBITDA margin 13.5% to 14.3%; EPS SEK 2.30, 2.71, 3.23. Against the change forces, the 2026-28 path is credible and slightly conservative on Data Center revenue and Fiber Solutions margin, but it does not price the risks of a US refill ending or a stronger krona; the 2029 step (EBIT margin 12.1%, EPS +29%) rests on one broker and on falling D&A and is the least credible.

  • Main lines (FactSet median, 22 October 2026): revenue SEK 8,512m / 9,345m / 10,008m / 10,791m (2026-29); EBITDA SEK 1,147m / 1,280m / 1,430m / 1,582m; EBIT SEK 734m / 884m / 994m / 1,306m; EPS SEK 2.30 / 2.71 / 3.23 / 4.18; capex SEK 163m / 264m / 279m; free cash flow SEK 70m / 512m / 636m; dividend zero. Like-for-like growth from the FY2025 brokers'-basis base: revenue +12.9%, +9.8%, +7.1%, +7.8%; EBITDA +42.7%, +11.6%, +11.7%, +10.6%.
  • Against history: revenue grew about 45% a year in 2019-23 and fell 4% a year in 2023-25; brokers'-basis EBITDA margin was about 17.9% (2023), 14.4% (2024), 10.7% (2025). Consensus lands margins back near 2024 levels by 2027 and short of 2023.
  • Granular (FactSet segment consensus): Fiber Solutions SEK 4,968m / 5,288m / 5,562m; Data Center SEK 2,089m / 2,448m / 2,674m; Harsh Environment SEK 1,400m / 1,559m / 1,645m. Data Center 2026 is likely about SEK 2.2bn after Q3, so 2027 at SEK 2.45bn (+11%) is conservative unless normalisation is severe; Fiber Solutions growth of 6-7% in 2027 is plausible; Harsh Environment is plausible.
  • Profit check: the covering broker's 2027 EBITA of about SEK 1,024m (11.0% margin) sits within my run-rate range of SEK 1.0-1.1bn; EPS SEK 2.71 against my SEK 2.6-3.0 range.
  • Post-Q3 adjustments to expect: FY2026 EPS up to about SEK 2.35-2.45 (reported), 2027 up 0-5%; the covering broker has said it will raise forecasts "slightly" and that a higher risk-free rate (3% from 2.5%) offsets part of the valuation benefit.
  • What the forecasts omit: krona translation (a 5-10% stronger krona removes 4-9% of reported revenue); a US refill ending; supply caps on volume.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C65

Judgement. Hexatronic's record against estimates is cyclical: it beat in the 2021-22 boom, missed through the 2023-25 bust, and has beaten in four of the last five quarters in the 2026 upswing. In comparable conditions (an upswing with a lowered cost base), the record favours beats; the risk is that the same pattern reverses quickly when the cycle turns, as it did in 2023.

  • Last year (sales against consensus; profit noted): Q3 2025 +6%; Q4 2025 +11% (EBITA beat); Q1 2026 -6% on sales (cold winter) but EBITA SEK 146m against SEK 140m and EPS SEK 0.45 against SEK 0.30; Q2 2026 +11% sales, +10-12% EBITA, EPS SEK 0.61 against SEK 0.49; Q3 2026 +11% sales (SEK 2,498m against SEK 2,256m), +21% EBITA (SEK 281m against SEK 233m), +37% EPS (SEK 0.81 against SEK 0.59).
  • Last three years: 2024 mixed (Q1 -5%, Q2 +8%, Q3 in line, Q4 -2%); 2025 Q1 -1%, Q2 -4% with a profit warning; the covering broker cut 2024-25 EBITA by up to 13% and 2025-27 EBITA by 16-18% during 2025; FY2026 EPS consensus fell from SEK 5.05 to SEK 1.76 before recovering.
  • Last five years: strong beats in 2021-22; H1 2023 strong, then a two-year downgrade cycle.
  • Comparable environment: the current upswing resembles 2021 more than 2023 in direction, but with a smaller, more diversified base and supply constraints.
C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

C60

Judgement. Yes, estimates were cut deeply and fully in 2023-25, and guidance was rebased and the base kitchen-sinked in September 2025; that rebasing is complete and estimates have been rising for twelve months. The cushion that a low base provides has therefore been partly used: the 2026 base is no longer depressed, and from here estimates must be driven by delivery rather than by recovery from a sandbagged starting point.

  • The cut: FY2026 EPS consensus from SEK 5.05 to a low of SEK 1.76 (-65%); the covering broker's base case from SEK 57 (early 2025) to SEK 24 (September 2025); share price low of about SEK 16-17.
  • The rebasing: long-term FTTH outlook softened and group targets replaced (September 2025); SEK 298m of non-recurring costs in 2025; SEK 67m of improvement costs excluded in late 2025; the FTTH metric moved to homes passed. Sceptics called it kitchen-sinking; the subsequent over-delivery is consistent with that reading.
  • The recovery: FY2026 EPS mean now about SEK 2.26 (+12% over six months, +14% over twelve); FY2027 +15-16% over twelve months; FY2028 +27%; target price +36% over six months; buy share 75%; one-month and three-month revisions now flat (0-1%), so the upgrade round is paused pending Q3 follow-through.

Stock market narrative

C601 question
C-2260

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The market narrative has moved from "busted FTTH duct maker" to "data-centre and US fibre recovery story, half told" (Sector Digest), and the shares (+113% over twelve months, at their own ten-year median multiple) already reflect the recovery. The staircase ahead is adequate rather than rich: the next six months offer results-driven steps (a record year, an easy Q1 2027 comparison, management appointments, more bolt-ons), the middle period depends on proving that Data Center's normalised level and US growth hold against hard comparisons, and the one high-quality novel step (subsea capacity in 2028) is far out. The narrative risk is that the market reads Q4 2026 Data Center normalisation or a slowing US growth rate as the end of the story; the narrative opportunity is that the business proves it can grow through 2027 with a structurally higher margin floor, which would support the mix-change argument for a higher multiple.

C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

C60

Judgement. The staircase of novel, dated positive developments is decent in the next six months (driven by results against easy or beatable comparisons and by appointments), thinner in months six to eighteen (BABA-compliant cable, BEAD revenue, Data Center proving its normalised level), and contains one high-quality, contracted step in months eighteen to thirty-six (the Hudiksvall subsea line in 2028). Most steps are already discussed, so novelty is moderate; the most novel would be a resumption of capital returns, which management has not signalled.

  • Within 6 months (to April 2027):
  • Q4 2026 report (early February 2027): first record sales year above the 2023 peak (about SEK 8.6bn against SEK 8.15bn), Fiber Solutions margin at or near its 10% target, the size of Data Center normalisation. Novelty medium; well-founded medium-high.
  • Permanent CFO and Data Center head appointments. Novelty low; well-founded medium.
  • Further bolt-on acquisitions at 5-7x. Novelty low; well-founded high.
  • Q1 2027 report (late April 2027) against a very easy comparison (Q1 2026 sales SEK 1,698m, adjusted EBITA SEK 146m, organic -2%, cold winter): strong year-on-year growth in a seasonally weak quarter is likely. Novelty medium; well-founded high.
  • BEAD subgrantee contracts converting into first vendor orders. Novelty low-medium; well-founded medium.
  • Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028):
  • South Carolina fibre-cable line (2027) making Hexatronic's cable BABA-compliant for subsidised contracts. Novelty medium; well-founded medium (history of commissioning slips).
  • BEAD construction revenue ramping; continued US growth against hard comparisons. Novelty low; well-founded medium.
  • Data Center sustaining 15-20% organic growth on a normalised base; Harsh Environment margin recovery. Novelty medium; well-founded medium-low.
  • Deleveraging toward 1x and possible updated targets or a capital markets day (none announced). Novelty medium; well-founded low.
  • Months 18 to 36 (2028 to 2029):
  • Hudiksvall subsea line operational (capacity from about SEK 150-200m to above SEK 500m a year; NKT framework to 2032). Novelty high; well-founded medium-high (contracted partner; permit risk).
  • Delivery against the 2028 targets (SEK 11bn; about 12% margin). Novelty low; well-founded medium.
  • Glass supply normalising as new preform capacity lands, easing input costs. Novelty medium; well-founded medium (it also removes scarcity pricing across the chain).
  • Dividend reinstatement. Novelty high; well-founded low (policy is acquisitions first).
