  • Amortisation (SEK 100m, 112m, 122m, 132m, 140m pre-tax) is an intended basis difference, anticipated by the second set; it does not show that an input is wrong. The Consumer must compare the cross-check EPS, not the model's, with consensus, and must apply any price-to-earnings multiple drawn from the consensus-basis history (the dashboard's 13.8x and its ten-year percentiles) to the cross-check EPS, not to the model's.
  • The revenue base is a mechanical artefact: the model built FY2026 as FactSet's H1 2026 quarters plus Q3 and Q4 2025 on FactSet's quarterly basis (SEK 1,981m and 1,999m) grown at the Part 3 rates (32.7% and 20.7%), giving about SEK 8,990m, against the company's Q3 and Q4 2025 of SEK 1,883m and 1,848m, which give the second set's SEK 8,670m. FactSet's four 2025 quarters sum to SEK 7,794m against its own annual SEK 7,245m, so the quarterly history is not on the annual basis. The error then compounds through the annual growth rates: revenue 3.6% too high in every year, EBITA SEK 38m (2026) to SEK 67m (2030) too high, and the "+24.0%" growth for FY2026 is meaningless. No forecast input is wrong; the Financial Analyst should rebase the quarterly history to the company's figures (or build FY2026 from the Part 1 annual rate of 15.3% on the company's SEK 7,519m) before the memo is written. I have not altered the growth inputs to compensate, because that would misstate the growth the company actually reported.
  • Free cash flow, FY2026 (model SEK 285m against the cross-check SEK 455m). The model's figure reconstructs as net income SEK 575m plus depreciation SEK 290m less a working-capital build of SEK 330m (24.0% of its inflated revenue, SEK 2,157m, against FactSet's end-2025 balance of SEK 1,827m) less capex SEK 140m and leases SEK 110m. The cross-check implies a working-capital build of only about SEK 125m. The SEK 205m difference has two parts. About SEK 75m is the revenue base artefact. About SEK 130m is a definitional mismatch: the 24.0% ratio is a correct estimate of the year-end balance-sheet ratio, but the model treats the whole change in that balance as an operating outflow, including the receivables and inventories that arrived with JOWO and Superior (about SEK 550m of annualised revenue, so about SEK 130m of working capital) and that were already paid for inside the SEK 560m acquisition line. The company's own nine-month working-capital outflow was SEK 205m, and Q4 is the seasonal release quarter (Q4 2025 released about SEK 110-145m), so a full-year operating build of about SEK 125m is the right order of magnitude; the cross-check's SEK 455m is therefore closer to the truth than the model's SEK 285m, though I put FY2026 free cash flow before acquisitions at SEK 420-460m rather than above it, because the supply constraint is adding inventory. The same mismatch recurs each year at about SEK 100m (SEK 450m of acquired revenue at about 22%), which is most of the SEK 71-83m free-cash-flow gaps in 2027-30. This does show that an input, as the model uses it, is wrong: I lower the working-capital ratio (section on inputs; inputs-changed.md) so that the model's cash flow follows the judgement, and I say there how to restore the balance-sheet ratio if the model is instead changed to carry acquired working capital inside the acquisition line.

One further point of congruence the Consumer should carry into the memo. The dashboard's Eight Master Ratings as of 22 October (Change Forces B, Foundations C, Fit B, Riskiness C, Sell-side C, Thematic D, Technical B, TSR/Valuation D) are consistent with the first two sets' scores (strong external and internal change forces, mediocre economics and industry position, moderate riskiness) and with what this set finds: the case is a well-understood recovery with a cyclical core, not a compounder.

D-02

Plausibility of the expected change on demonstrated strengths

C60

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement. The change the base case expects over the next 18 months (revenue about SEK 9.7bn in 2027, an adjusted EBITA margin of about 11%, reported EPS of about SEK 2.8) is plausible on strengths the business has demonstrated in the last eighteen months, and the base case was built so as to rely on them: a cost base cut by SEK 120m and shown in three consecutive quarters of Fiber Solutions margin (5.2% to 9.4%); a direct US sales model that produced 44% and 51% North American growth while the distributor-led peer (Clearfield) was cutting its outlook; a Data Center business that doubled quarterly sales while holding a 15-17% margin and absorbing a 12.5%-margin acquisition; three bolt-ons bought at 4.7-6.4x EBITDA and performing ahead of the covering broker's expectations; an equity raise executed near the highs; and four profit beats in five quarters against the company's own consensus. What the base case does not rely on, and the 2028 targets do, are strengths the business would have to acquire: a Fiber Solutions margin that holds near 10% once the refill ends and comparisons harden; a Harsh Environment margin of 15% from 11%; secured glass and connector supply; a permanent CFO and a permanent Data Center head; a subsea line commissioned on time by a company with four commissioning slips; and an acquisition engine that keeps its cadence and multiples without its architect. The plausibility report (t-d1) and the sector precedents support this split precisely: cost cuts stabilise the trough and volume sets the peak (Prysmian, Orbia, Belden), post-destock margin recoveries at duct makers have not held through year two without pricing (Dura-Line's duct prices fell in 2024 as volumes rose), service scaling by product companies has held margins only where product pull-through was strong (Belden to about 17% EBITDA; Dycom's Building Systems at 24.5%), and the harsh-environment margin step has precedents only in pure connector businesses (Bel Fuse's Enercon integration lifting gross margin to 39-41%; Amphenol at 27-30%) rather than in cable-heavy mixes like Hexatronic's (Nexans' subsea transmission at 13.7%; Siemens Energy offshore targeting 8-10%). So the 18-month forecasts rest on demonstrated strengths; the 2028-30 forecasts rest about one third on strengths to be acquired, and the company's 12% target rests about half on them.

Demonstrated, with the evidence that it has been demonstrated in Hexatronic's own numbers.

  • Cost discipline and absorption. SEK 120m delivered against SEK 110m, ahead of schedule; Fiber Solutions margin 5.4% (Q3 2025) to 9.4% (Q3 2026) with the first two steps from the programme and the last two from North American volume. The management cues on cost and pricing since late 2025 have been accurate (own-4). The structural floor the second set assumes (8-9% on flat volume) is a demonstrated property of the lower cost base, not a hope.
  • US direct model and local capacity. Share gains through the 2024-25 downturn; the Clinton reversal; frame agreements (Novos SEK 400m over three years); Clearfield's backlog down 34% sequentially to June 2026 while Hexatronic's order book held at four months. The channel-check report (own-2) concludes the 2026 surge is predominantly end-use (data-centre transport and PE-funded builds) with restocking a secondary component; distributor inventories are described as balanced.
  • Data Center execution. Organic growth 37% (2025), 20%, 27%, 44% (Q1-Q3 2026); margin 15.3-16.9% through five quarters of doubling; Communication Zone delivered a material project ahead of expectations; Superior broadened the customer base. Precedents say installation businesses can earn high-teens to low-twenties margins when demand is this strong (Dycom).
  • Acquisition discipline. 4.7-6.4x EBITDA with earn-outs; founders retained; both 2026 deals ahead of plan in Q3. Nordic precedents (Lifco 17 deals in 2025; Lagercrantz 7-9 a year; HMS 17 since 2012 at about 9.7x EV/EBIT) show the model works for small targets through cycles.
  • Capital markets access and credibility. SEK 600m raised at SEK 38 on an oversubscribed book; four beats; leverage 1.3x.

Would need to be acquired, and how much of the forecasts depends on each.

  • Holding the Fiber Solutions margin as growth slows (2027-28). No duct maker in the files held a recovered margin through year two without price: Dura-Line's EBITDA margin went from 33% (early 2023) to 15.7% (2025) and its 2026 volume recovery comes with conduit price pressure; Clearfield's gross margin went 35.7% to 13.7% to 31.8%. Hexatronic's own Q4 2025 (volume up, price down, margin 5.2%) is the internal precedent. The base case needs 9.4% in 2027 and 9.8% in 2028 with North American growth of 5-10% from Q2 2027 and duct price flat to down; that is plausible on absorption alone but it is the least demonstrated of the 18-month assumptions. Dependence: about SEK 50-100m of 2027-28 EBITA, 5-8%.
  • Harsh Environment to 15%. From 11% with dynamic cables depressed and Rochester under-invested; JOWO itself is about 11% adjusted EBITA after its 14% EBITDA. The base case does not rely on this (12.0%, 13.0%, 14.0%, 14.5%); the 12% group target does. Precedents exist only where the mix is connectors.
  • Secured supply. New-supplier validation takes about six months (own-5); the company began validating in Q3 2026, so relief from new suppliers arrives in H1 2027 at the earliest, and the structural relief (new preform capacity) in 2028. The base case already caps 2027 organic growth at 9%; the upside scenario (SEK 3.2 of 2027 EPS) requires this acquired strength.
  • Leadership depth. Permanent CFO and Data Center head by February 2027 is the test. The base case assumes no execution slip from the vacancies; the record of the acting Data Center head (Q3 2026) is good.
  • Subsea commissioning. Four prior slips (Clinton 2022, South Carolina 2023, Ogden 2024, cable line to 2027) and a permit still pending; the base case's SEK 100m in 2028 is the exposed figure.
  • Acquisition cadence without the architect. The base case needs SEK 400m a year; the departure of Martin Åberg (nine-month notice) and the acting head doubling as Head of M&A thin the bench; DNB Carnegie's view that sub-EUR 50m competition is undemanding supports the cadence in Europe, while US data-centre installer multiples (6.9-9.7x in 2026) threaten it there.

Against the common view. The plausibility report's own net read, "medium-to-high confidence" that the 2028 targets are reachable, is more generous than mine, because it credits the Harsh Environment step on connector-peer precedents and treats Data Center's Q3 margin as proof that services do not dilute; I read both as mix effects that reverse when product timing turns, and I give the SEK 11bn at 12% target about a one-in-three chance (revenue reachable with acquisitions, margin not), which is why the second set's 11.3% peak and the incentive range are the right anchors.

D-03

Concentration or diffusion of the drivers that separate the forecasts from the last four to six years

❌ D48

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement. The forecasts differ from 2020-25 in two things: a growth path of about 11% a year that is half acquired and a quarter services, instead of a boom-and-bust swing from +88% to -7%; and a margin that plateaus near 11% instead of travelling from 5% to 16.6% and back to 8.4%. Counted internally, the drivers of those differences are diffuse (eight segment and below-the-line items of which no single one exceeds about a third of the 2027 EBITA difference against 2025); counted externally, they are concentrated, because about three quarters of the organic part of the difference runs through two demand channels that are themselves correlated, US private-carrier and PE-builder spending and hyperscaler spending, both of which are financed at the long end of the yield curve and both of which the AI-capex narrative moves at once. The remaining quarter runs through things Hexatronic controls or has contracted (the cost base, acquisitions at known multiples, the NKT framework, falling interest) and through one constraint it does not control (glass and connector allocation). The structure is therefore more diffuse than 2020-25, when a single driver (European and US FTTH duct volume with its price) explained nearly everything, but less diffuse than the segment count suggests, and the two channels can fail together: a discount-rate shock that pauses PE-funded fibre builds would also be the shock that pauses marginal AI financing (the CoreWeave bond rout and Oracle's downgrade in 2026 show the channel is live). For predictability this is a moderate negative: the forecasts are easier to monitor than in 2022 (two channels, both observable through peers), but a single adverse turn moves EBITA 15-25%.

The drivers, ranked by their share of the difference between the 2027 base case and 2025 (adjusted EBITA SEK 1,057m against SEK 632m, a difference of about SEK 425m), with the external channel each depends on.

  1. North American Fiber Solutions volume absorption (about SEK 120-150m of the difference, 30-35%; channel: US carrier and PE-builder capex, BEAD from 2027). Incremental margin 30-40%; ±10% volume = ±SEK 160-200m of 2027 EBITA. The most concentrated and the most volatile item: the driver report (t-d2) records that North American sales fell 26% year on year in late 2025 when a handful of regional customers deferred orders, and the channel-check report (own-2) records Brightspeed's 2027 liquidity cliff (USD 5.6bn burned against a USD 3bn budget), Ting's move to capital-light, and AT&T's USD 1bn lower capex for 2027-28, against Metronet, Lumos, Novos and GoNetspeed being fully funded. Refill versus use is undecomposed by anyone; the best evidence (distributor inventories balanced; Dura-Line +30% with conduit price pressure) says mostly use with a refill fraction that will wash out in 2027.
  2. The cost programme (about SEK 90-100m year on year into 2026, nil thereafter; 20-25% of the difference; internal, already delivered). Fully predictable.
  3. Data Center growth (about SEK 60-80m, 15-20%; channel: hyperscaler and enterprise capex, with services about 39%). Lower leverage (15-25%) but lumpier: one Communication Zone project drove much of Q3 2026; MasTec deferred USD 400m when AT&T paused; Dycom deferred USD 150m. Direct AI cloud sales are less than a third of the segment (Redeye), the rest enterprise, education and government, which diversifies but does not remove the channel.
  4. Acquired units (SEK 225m of 2027 revenue at 12%, about SEK 27m of EBITA plus the annualisation of 2026 deals, together about SEK 60-70m, 15%; internal, conditional on the share price staying above about 9-10x forward earnings and on bandwidth).
  5. Depreciation dilution (about 70 basis points of margin from 2025 to 2027, SEK 50-70m; arithmetic, reverses in 2028-29).
  6. Harsh Environment mix (defence up, dynamic cables flat; about SEK 50m; channels: German budget execution, which ran 30% below plan year to date in 2026, and offshore FIDs, expected +30% in 2027).
  7. Europe not falling further (-3% in 2027 against -9% to -31% in 2026; worth about SEK 30-40m against a continued decline; channel: UK and DACH operator capex, structurally weak).
  8. Below the line (net interest down about SEK 45m from 2025 to 2027, tax normalising, share count up 8%): predictable; amortisation and earn-out revaluations add noise, not direction.

The correlation that concentrates it. Items 1 and 3 share a financing channel (US long rates and the AI-capex appetite), a geography (48% of sales in North America), a currency (the krona against the dollar) and a supply constraint (glass and connectors). The first set's probabilities (US refill ending 20-25%; a hyperscaler pause 15-20%) are not independent; I put the joint probability of at least one over 18 months at about 35% and of both at about 10%. Against that, items 2, 4, 5 and 8 are known or contracted, which is why the 2027 cone is narrower than 2023's and why the downside case is a 25% EPS fall rather than the 100% fall of 2023-25.

Against the last cycle and against peers. In 2021-23 one driver (FTTH duct volume and price in Europe and the US, with 100% decremental margins) explained the whole swing; the dispersion of outcomes was extreme (Hexatronic's FY2026 EPS consensus from SEK 5.05 to SEK 1.76; Clearfield's gross margin 35.7% to 13.7%; Dura-Line's EBITDA margin 33% to 15.7%). Now three cycles that do not move together (US fibre, data centre, defence and energy) carry the revenue, and acquisitions add a fourth leg. Peer precedent for the second year after a destock recovery is mixed: the Americas 2012-13 correction was followed by several years of about 7% growth; the 2023-24 US correction ended within about three years; the Chinese 2018-21 cycle halved prices in 18 months once capacity landed. The driver report's judgement, moderate concentration with a wide, near-term positively skewed range and material tail risks, matches mine except on skew (D-04).

D-04

The width and skew of the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months

❌ D44

Question asked How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

Judgement. The cone is narrow for the next two reports and wide thereafter, and its skew turns from positive to negative at the same point, so the 18-month window divides into a six-month period in which the forecasts are quite predictable and likely to be beaten, and a twelve-month period in which they are not. For Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 the base case (sales SEK 2,230m and 2,090m; adjusted EBITA SEK 225m and 211m) sits inside a range of roughly ±5% on sales and ±12% on EBITA, because the order book covers about four months, Data Center's Q4 normalisation has been pre-announced and bounded (SEK 500-600m against SEK 550m assumed; Redeye SEK 582m), submarine deliveries are guided flat, and Q1 2027 meets the weakest comparison in the company's recent history (SEK 1,698m, organic -2%, cold winter). From Q2 2027 the comparisons harden (North America +44% and +51%), the refill question is answered one way or the other, the hyperscaler financing question may be answered too, and the range on full-year 2027 reported EPS is about SEK 2.1 to SEK 3.3 around a base of SEK 2.78 (-25% to +18%), with probability weights I put at 55% base, 27% downside and 18% upside, giving a weighted SEK 2.7 and a modestly negative skew. For 2028 the range is SEK 2.6 to SEK 3.9 around SEK 3.29, with the skew more clearly negative because the capacity cycle and the 2029 consensus artefact sit at the far end. On top of the earnings cone sits a share-price cone that is wider still and asymmetric for reasons unrelated to earnings (D-07 and C-82): a trough multiple of 8-10x on intact earnings implies 25-40% downside; a sustained re-rating toward the ten-year 75th percentile (17.6x) implies about 30% upside. The driver report's finding that the near-term range is "positively skewed" is right for the next two prints; my view that the 18-month range is negatively skewed rests on the hardening comparisons, the open downside channels and the capped upside, and on the company's own 2023 precedent, in which the turn arrived faster than the forecasts.

Why the near term is narrow. Three things bound it. Management's cues have been under-statements for a year (July 2026's margin warning preceded an 11.3% margin). The two quarters are mechanically favoured: Q4 2026 is compared with a SEK 1,848m quarter at 7.2%, and Q1 2027 with a SEK 1,698m quarter at 8.6%, so even a sequentially soft Q4 (Redeye 9.6% margin) shows year-on-year EBITA growth of about 60-70%, and a Q1 2027 of SEK 2,038-2,090m shows 20-23% growth and a doubling of margin on the year. And the lumps are known: Data Center's project timing, submarine shipments (flat), Harsh Environment's dynamic cables (flat). The residual risks are a glass or connector allocation cut large enough to defer shipments (10-15% probability of losing SEK 150-200m of revenue in a quarter), a sharper-than-guided Data Center drop (below SEK 500m), and the year-end items that make Q4 noisy (inventory reserves, bonuses, earn-out revaluations of ±SEK 30m, the SEK 212m obsolescence reserve as a key audit matter).

Why the twelve months after are wide, item by item (2027 reported EPS of SEK 2.78 as the base).

  • North American Fiber Solutions volume from Q2 2027: range -20% to +20%; ±SEK 0.55-0.65 of EPS per 10% of Fiber Solutions volume. The downside trigger is observable early (North American growth in single digits before the comparisons harden; Brightspeed's 2027 refinancing; BEAD's technology mix at 62% fibre, 24% satellite, 13% fixed wireless). The upside trigger (BEAD orders from mid-2027; validated suppliers) is slower.
  • Data Center organic growth: range 5-25% against 15%; ±SEK 0.15-0.20 per 10 points. Single-project concentration (Communication Zone) and the hyperscaler financing channel (CoreWeave yields above 16% in mid-October; Oracle at BBB-) make the downside abrupt; peer contractors defer hundreds of millions on one customer decision.
  • Price and gross margin: the two-way lag. If G.652.D keeps falling, 2027 gross margin gets a 50-100 basis-point tailwind for a quarter or two; if duct overcapacity deepens, Fiber Solutions gives back margin as 2024-25. Net ±SEK 0.10-0.15.
  • Currency: a 10% krona move is ±9% of revenue and ±3% of EPS (±SEK 0.08); the Riksbank is expected to raise in November 2026, and the base case already carries -2% translation.
  • Acquisitions and below the line: the SEK 400m block adds about SEK 0.05 of 2027 EPS and could be zero if the share de-rates; earn-out revaluations and items affecting comparability add ±SEK 0.10-0.20 of reported-EPS noise with a negative skew (the record is SEK 134m to SEK 298m of one-offs a year in 2024-25; a defensive European consolidation would cost SEK 100-150m).

Historical dispersion as a check. The driver report assembles the precedents: Hexatronic's own FY2026 EPS consensus travelled from SEK 5.05 to SEK 1.76 and back above SEK 2.3 in two years; Clearfield's gross margin fell 22 points and recovered 18; Dura-Line's EBITDA margin halved; HMS Networks' organic orders fell 36% in one quarter; Chinese fibre prices fell 40% in 2019-20 when capacity landed. Against those, a ±25% range on year-ahead EPS is tight, and that is a fair reading of what diversification and the lower cost base have done. But the precedents also say forecasts resting on one or two volume drivers were met in the second year after a destock recovery less often than not (Clearfield's fiscal 2026 cut; MasTec's and Dycom's deferrals; Hexatronic's own 2024-25 cuts), which is why I weight the downside above the upside.

What narrows the cone fastest. In order of information value: Corning's and Prysmian's Q3 and Q4 commentary on carrier and enterprise shipments (two quarters' lead on Hexatronic's inventory and volume cycle, per own-4); G.652.D and G.657.A2 spot prices and connector lead times (one to two quarters' lead on gross margin and on the end of scarcity); hyperscaler 2027 capex guidance in late January and February 2027 (three to six months' lead on Data Center); Hexatronic's own North American growth rate and Data Center level at the 5 February 2027 report; and the Q1 2027 report, which is the last easy quarter. By late April 2027 the Consumer will know which side of the cone the year is on.

D-05

Helpfulness of the company's disclosure for monitoring the case over about six months

C52

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement. The disclosure is good on outcomes and poor on leading indicators, so it lets the Consumer verify the thesis one quarter at a time but not anticipate the turn. What is helpful is unusually complete for a company this size: three segments reported quarterly with net sales, organic growth and adjusted EBITA; a geographic split; a company-commissioned consensus published before each print, which makes beats and misses unambiguous; qualitative cues for the coming quarter that have been reliable for a year on cost, pricing, seasonality and regions; an order-book duration (about four months); cash conversion and adjusted leverage. What is missing is exactly what would make the next six months predictable rather than merely verifiable: order intake and book-to-bill (Clearfield reports its backlog to the dollar; Dycom reports backlog and the share to be completed within twelve months), backlog ageing, a Fiber Solutions order book separate from the submarine-skewed group figure, the split of price and volume, the size of price increases, project-level submarine timing, services renewal rates and the standalone margins of acquired units. The consequence is that the two variables that decide 2027 (North American Fiber Solutions volume after the refill; the sustainable Data Center level) can only be read from the reported quarters and from peers, with a lag of one to two quarters relative to when the turn happens. The disclosure report (own-4) also shows that management's cues are not equally reliable: cues on cost savings, pricing pass-through, seasonality and regional inflections since late 2025 predicted the next one or two quarters well, while demand-recovery cues in 2023-24 ("inventories normalised" in mid-2024; "recovery in H2 2024") lagged the market by a year and preceded downgrades. The Consumer should therefore weight management's statements about its own actions and costs heavily and its statements about customer demand lightly, and should build the six-month monitoring plan around external leads.

What to watch, and when, over the next six months.

  • 5 February 2027 (Q4 2026 and the annual report): Data Center sales against SEK 500-600m (my base SEK 550m; Redeye SEK 582m); North American Fiber Solutions growth (below 20% would signal the refill ending early); Fiber Solutions margin above or below 9% (Redeye models 8.0% for Q4); group adjusted EBITA against the four-contributor consensus (likely about SEK 215-225m); working capital at the year-end and cash conversion; the status of the glass and connector constraint (lead times, any lost revenue); the permanent CFO and Data Center appointments; the Hudiksvall permit; the LTIP 2026 base-year EPS. The annual report is also the first place the standalone contribution of Superior and Communication Zone may be visible.
  • Late April 2027 (Q1 2027): group organic growth against the easy base (above 15% is the base case; below 10% is the downside); Data Center against SEK 500-540m (Redeye SEK 536m); the first signs of BEAD orders; any change of tone on Q2 and Q3 comparisons.
  • External leads, in order of lead time (own-4): fibre and connector lead times and G.652.D spot prices (about six to nine months ahead of gross margin and organic growth; a fall in lead times preceded the 2023 crash by about nine months); Corning's carrier and enterprise shipment commentary (about two quarters ahead of Hexatronic's inventory cycle; Q3 results in late October 2026 and Q4 in late January 2027); hyperscaler capex guidance (three to six months ahead of Data Center; late January to early February 2027); Dura-Line and Orbia conduit volumes and pricing (same cycle); Clearfield's backlog and community-broadband commentary (early February); Dycom's and MasTec's fiscal 2027 outlooks and deferrals; state BEAD award trackers (Louisiana, Indiana, Pennsylvania are furthest advanced); the FTTH Council Europe build data (about six months ahead of Fiber Solutions Europe); the krona (a Riksbank hike in November would show as a translation drag from Q4).
  • What the disclosure will not tell the Consumer in time: a US customer deferral (late 2025's 26% fall was visible only when reported); a hyperscaler pause (visible first in peer contractors' deferrals); a supplier allocation cut (visible only as lost revenue); the refill-versus-use split (never disclosed; inferred from distributor commentary).

Compared with peers. Hexatronic's segment-level organic growth is more granular than Prysmian's or Nexans' telecom disclosure, and its pre-print consensus is a practice few peers follow; its forward disclosure is weaker than Clearfield's (backlog), Dycom's (backlog and conversion) and Corning's (carrier against enterprise lines and multi-year agreements). HMS Networks discloses orders and order intake, which is why its consensus moves earlier. Hexatronic's four-month order book is skewed by long-duration submarine orders and does not separate Fiber Solutions, so it can misstate near-term FTTH trends (Redeye, July 2026).

Net. For a six-month horizon dominated by two quarters whose outcomes are bounded, the disclosure is adequate to confirm or refute the base case at each print and good enough to catch a large miss early (the consensus is public; the segments are visible). It is not adequate to anticipate the second-half 2027 turn, which must be read from peers and prices. That is a middling result: more helpful than the average Nordic small-cap, less helpful than a company that wants its investors to see the order book.

D-06

Traceability from what the company says it will achieve to EPS, and the clarity of the external transmission

C55

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement. The internal transmission is clear in structure and opaque in two links; the external transmission is clear in direction and uncertain in timing and magnitude at every link. Internally, the company's stated destination (SEK 11bn of sales and about 12% EBITA in 2028, with segment targets of SEK 6bn at 10%, SEK 3bn at 15% and SEK 2bn at 15%, acquisitions included) can be laid onto its reported lines one to one, and from segment EBITA to EPS the chain is mechanical and well disclosed: group adjusted EBITA, less amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 100m rising to SEK 140m), less net financial items (SEK 132m falling to SEK 65m, with ±SEK 30m a quarter of earn-out revaluation noise), tax at about 26% (nine months 2026 at 26.7%), about 223m diluted shares in 2027 drifting up by 1m a year. The two opaque links are the organic-versus-acquired split of the SEK 11bn (the company does not state it; the second set assumes SEK 1.1bn of unannounced acquisitions by 2028) and the path, since the company gives no annual numbers and its incentive plan pays on an average margin of 9.8-11.1% while the target says about 12%. Externally, the transmission from value-chain dynamics to EPS is clear in sign at every link but has a lag and a leverage coefficient that are only approximately known: US carrier and PE-builder capex to North American Fiber Solutions volume (lag one to two quarters; the order book is four months) to Fiber Solutions EBITA (incremental margin 30-40%); hyperscaler capex to Data Center revenue (lag three to six months; project timing adds ±SEK 100m a quarter) to Data Center EBITA (incremental margin 15-25%, higher than the second set assumed because services are 39%, not half); glass and connector prices to gross margin (one-quarter lag, both ways, with the Q3 2026 margin showing pass-through working); the krona to revenue and EPS (-9% and -3% per 10%, immediate); German defence budget execution to JOWO (lag unknown; spending ran 30% below plan year to date); offshore FIDs to dynamic cables (lag of one to two years; 2027 FIDs +30%); the capacity cycle to pricing (from late 2027, with the first effect positive for input costs and the second negative for scarcity pricing). What the Consumer cannot do is trace from what the company says it will achieve to EPS without supplying the acquisitions block and the margin path from outside, and cannot trace from value-chain data to a quarter's EPS without accepting ±SEK 0.10-0.15 of noise from one-offs and revaluations. For a monitoring purpose that is good enough; for a forecasting purpose it leaves the two decisive assumptions (North American volume after Q1 2027; the sustainable Data Center level) outside the company's own statements.

The internal chain, with the milestones the Consumer can tick.

What the company says The reported line it lands on The milestone to watch Status at Q3 2026
Fiber Solutions SEK 6bn at 10% through the cycle Segment sales and adjusted EBITA margin Margin at or above 9% in Q4 2026; North American growth; Europe stabilising SEK 1,414m a quarter; 9.4%; Europe -9%
Data Center SEK 3bn at 15% Segment sales and margin Q4 2026 level against first-half pattern (SEK 434-538m); margin 15-17% SEK 692m; 16.9%; rolling twelve months above SEK 2bn
Harsh Environment SEK 2bn at 15% Segment sales and margin Dynamic-cable orders; JOWO; Hudiksvall permit; margin above 12% SEK 393m; 11.0%; permit pending
Three to six acquisitions a year, under EUR 50m, 5-7x EBITDA Acquired growth; amortisation; contingent consideration in financial items Deal announcements; multiples; earn-out revaluations Three since November 2025; SEK 28m revaluation charge in Q3
Q4 2026 cues (Data Center normalising; Q4 and Q1 seasonally lower; submarine flat; US growth; Europe subdued; supply constraining) Q4 sales and margin by segment 5 February 2027 against the four-contributor consensus Cues have been conservative for a year
LTIP 2026: diluted EPS 40%, sales growth 25%, EBITA margin 25% (9.8-11.1% average 2026-28) Reported diluted EPS; group margin Average margin above 9.8% (vesting floor) and toward 11.1% (cap) 2026 about 10.1%
No numeric EPS, cash or capex guidance beyond a 2-4% capex-to-sales guideline Reported EPS; free cash flow None from the company; the second set's cross-check is the anchor Capex running at 1.5-3%

The external chain, with the coefficient and the lag. US private-carrier capex: about 80% of US fibre construction is private; a 10% change in North American Fiber Solutions volume (about SEK 260m) moves group EBITA by SEK 80-100m and EPS by about SEK 0.30 (lag one to two quarters). Hyperscaler capex: a 10-point change in Data Center organic growth (about SEK 265m) moves EBITA by SEK 45-65m and EPS by SEK 0.15-0.20 (lag three to six months). Glass: a doubling of the fibre input cost with a one-quarter pass-through lag costs about 50-100 basis points of gross margin for one quarter and then returns; a fall gives the same back. Krona: a Riksbank-driven 5% strengthening removes about 4.5% of reported revenue and 1.5% of EPS. Defence and offshore: slow and lumpy; the sign is positive for 2027-28 and the magnitude is small (Harsh Environment is 16% of sales). The capacity cycle: Chinese preform capacity of about 230m fibre-km by end-2027, Corning +50%, Prysmian doubling US fibre from 2028, Sterlite and HFCL expansions by 2028; the first effect (lower G.652.D cost) is already visible, the second (the end of data-centre scarcity pricing) is a 2028-29 event.

Where the clarity fails and what it costs. Three failures matter for the memo. First, the company's targets include acquisitions it has not made, so the "guidance" is not a forecast of the business as it stands; the Consumer should present the 2028 targets as a destination that requires roughly SEK 0.8-1.2bn of bought revenue. Second, consensus EPS, the LTIP's EPS measure and the model's EPS are on three bases (reported after amortisation; the plan's definition, not disclosed in the files; adjusted before amortisation), and the Q3 2026 impairment reversal sits inside the first; the memo must state its basis every time and should not let a pre-amortisation EPS meet a consensus-basis multiple. Third, the absence of order intake means that the first external signal of a turn will come from Corning, Prysmian, Dura-Line, Clearfield, Dycom and MasTec, not from Hexatronic; the Consumer should treat those six companies' quarterly commentary as part of Hexatronic's disclosure.

D-07

Co-movement of the share price and the multiple with sector and industry peers, and how far the multiple is company-driven

C55

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement. The share price has become almost entirely idiosyncratic relative to its listed sector peers and the multiple is overwhelmingly company-driven, with one important exception: on the days when the AI-optics cohort moves, Hexatronic moves with it, and those days produce the largest drawdowns. The computed co-movement table shows that over the latest six-month window (28 April to 23 October 2026) the dashboard's hardware basket explains 6% of Hexatronic's daily variance (correlation 0.25, beta 0.96), that no single peer explains more than 9% (HMS Networks 0.29 correlation; Cicor 0.25; Nokia 0.21; Ericsson 0.09), and that the share returned +9.0% while the basket returned -9.6%. The 24 months of month-end readings show the explained share falling from 18-23% in late 2024 and January 2025 (when Hexatronic traded as a high-beta hardware small-cap, beta 1.8-2.2) to 3-11% since mid-2025, as the company's own earnings cycle took over. The multiple tells the same story: 24-month-forward price-to-earnings of about 29x EV/EBIT in 2022, 5-6x at the 2024-25 trough, 13.4-13.8x now (the 52nd percentile of ten years, the 80th of two years, the 62nd of five), a journey that tracks diluted EPS from SEK 4.17 to SEK -0.07 to a recovery, not any sector multiple; peers in the dashboard sit at the 7th (Invisio, Kontron) to the 88th (Nokia) percentiles of their own histories with no common pattern. So the multiple is company-driven: it will follow Hexatronic's delivery and the market's belief in the durability of the recovery, which is good for predictability if the forecasts are met and bad if they are not, because the company's own precedent (2023) is that the multiple falls one to two quarters before earnings do. The exception is the cohort that the dashboard basket does not contain: Corning, Prysmian, Nokia, Amphenol, Fujikura and the optical names. The share-price report (own-3) documents that Corning's 28 July 2026 results (shares -20% on a guidance without upside) dragged optical names down 8-12% in sympathy, that the October 2026 "AI monetisation anxiety" sell-off took the optical and fibre cohort down 4.5% over three months, and that Hexatronic fell 18.7% from its July-August high on no company news (C-82). With about USD 5-7m of daily trading, one visible broker and no anchor owner, the share is thin enough that sympathy moves are exaggerated. For the next 18 months the practical reading is: the level of the multiple is set by Hexatronic's delivery against a cyclical-recovery template (fair range about 11-14x on the consensus basis for a mid-quality cyclical with a recent bust, higher only if 2027 proves the margin floor), while the path of the share price around that level is set by AI-optics sentiment and by Nordic small-cap flows, which can move it 15-25% in a quarter without any change in the forecasts.

The evidence in order of weight.

  • Statistical co-movement: R-squared against the 17-stock basket 6% now; trailing readings 20% (November 2024), 23% (January 2025), 9% (March 2025), 16% (May 2025), 4% (October 2025), 11% (June 2026), 10% (September 2026), 6% (October 2026). Beta has fallen from 2.2 to about 1.0 over the two years. The basket is a dashboard construct (Nordic and European hardware), so these figures measure co-movement with the listed sector rather than with the economic cohort; the economic cohort is mostly US-listed and not in the table.
  • Event co-movement with the economic cohort: Corning Q2 2026 (28 July) and the October 2026 AI-optics sell-off are the two episodes in the files; both moved Hexatronic without company news. Prysmian's 5% EBITDA miss (30 April 2026) is cited as a cohort event. The next such dates are Corning's Q3 (late October 2026), Prysmian's Q3 (29 October), hyperscaler results (late October and late January), and Corning's Q4 (late January 2027).
  • The multiple's own history: ten-year percentiles 10th 8.2x, 35th 11.7x, median 13.6x, 75th 17.6x, 90th 22.3x; two-year median 10.1x; the stock has been at 25x-plus only in the 2021-22 boom. Redeye's valuation framing has moved from a "35% discount to peers" (October 2025 and February 2026) to "9x EV/EBITA 2027e, no longer a deep discount" (October 2026).
  • Register and liquidity: Handelsbanken Fonder 5.81% (from 9.34%), Jonas Nordlund 4.91%, Accendo 4.38% (from 5.81%), Vanguard 3.68%, Tredje AP-fonden 2.92% (from 4.90%); the three largest Swedish institutions have reduced into the rally while international and passive holders have grown; insiders about 13%; 30-day average volume 1.29m shares. A register that is thinning at the top and broadening at the bottom is one that re-rates on delivery and de-rates on doubt.

Implication for the predictability of the case. A company-driven multiple means the forecasts and the share price are coupled through delivery, which is the right place for a recovery case to be: the Consumer can tie the share-price path to the milestones in D-05 and D-06. It also means the valuation downside is not insured by sector momentum: if Q2-Q3 2027 shows the refill ending, there is no sector multiple to hold the stock up, and the thin register and coverage mean the fall would be fast. The guideline fair multiple of 22.34x that the model applies at the 18-month column is the ten-year 90th percentile, a level this stock reached only when organic growth exceeded 60%; on the evidence of D-07 the company-driven multiple for a cyclical recovery that is "half told" sits in the 11.7-17.6x band (35th to 75th percentile) on consensus-basis EPS, and the memo Analyst should treat the 22.34x as the top of the range, not the central case, and apply whatever multiple is chosen to the cross-check EPS, not the model's pre-amortisation EPS.

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading Fourth set: The extent to which our assessment is reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D47 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The company's guidance, the sell-side's estimates and the market's narrative already reflect most of what we expect for the next 18 months, and what they do not reflect is weighted toward the cautious side of our assessment. On guidance, the qualitative cues are set to be beaten at the next two events (5 February and late April 2027) and the destination targets are set to be partly missed on margin and in Harsh Environment, but the one visible broker and the board's own incentive range already discount the 12% target, so the practical gap is small (I-01). On estimates, the post-Q3 Redeye figures sit at or slightly above our cross-check for 2026-28, so there is no upgrade cycle to be had from our base case; the near-term revision burst that the dashboard will record in November is the Q3 print being absorbed, the next two prints should nudge 2027 up by low single digits, and from mid-2027 the risk of cuts (40% at each of two events) exceeds the chance of upgrades; the one clear downside gap is the single-broker 2029 figure of SEK 4.18 against our SEK 3.58 (I-02). On narrative, the recovery story is two-thirds told, the dynamics that will decide 2027 (refill against use, the comparison cliff, services and duct pricing, the capacity cycle's two-way effect, the incentive range, the EPS basis) are articulated by one house lightly or by nobody, and the surprises left in the case are therefore mostly negative (I-03). Three steers for the memo. First, the favourable part of the gap is concentrated in the next six months and is about beats of a thin, company-set consensus, not about upgrades to annual estimates; the memo should say so and should not present the model summary's "+14-23% against consensus" as a view, because on a like-for-like, post-Q3 basis the gap is nil for 2026-28 (D-01, I-02). Second, the valuation work in topics #11 and #12 must use the cross-check EPS (consensus basis) with any multiple drawn from the consensus-basis history; the model's pre-amortisation EPS of SEK 4.28 for 2029 against a 22.34x guideline multiple (the ten-year 90th percentile) produces a value per share of SEK 95.52 that double-counts, once through the basis (the cross-check figure is SEK 3.58) and once through a multiple the stock reached only in the 2021-22 boom; on the consensus-basis EPS and the 35th-to-75th-percentile band (11.7-17.6x) the 18-month value is about SEK 42-63 against SEK 40.94 today, and the memo Analyst should give that range, with the guideline figure as the top of it. Third, the lifecycle reading that follows from all four sets is "thesis broadening but not yet reinforcing": change forces strong, delivery proven for eighteen months, the market two-thirds convinced, estimates caught up, the multiple at its own median, and the next twelve months the period in which the case either proves a structurally higher margin floor through hard comparisons or reverts to the cyclical template that the share's own history and the thin register would punish quickly.

Guidance

1 question
I-0156
I-01

The likelihood of guidance being raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events, and of the mid-term targets being raised, met or reduced

C56

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement. Hexatronic gives no numeric annual guidance, so "guidance" over the next 18 months means two things: the qualitative cues it gives for the coming quarter at each report, and the 2028 targets (SEK 11bn of sales; about 12% EBITA; the three segment targets, acquisitions included). On the cues, the pattern of the last four reports is that they are set conservatively and beaten, and I expect that to continue at the next two events and to stop being the pattern from the third. At the Q4 2026 report (5 February 2027) I put the probability that the quarter meets or beats the cues given on 22 October at about 70% (Data Center lands at SEK 530-600m rather than below the first-half pattern; US growth continues; Europe subdued as said; the supply constraint restricts rather than removes growth), and I expect the cues for Q1 2027 to be cautious again (seasonally low quarter; supply; Europe), which sets up a second beat in late April 2027 against the weakest comparison in the recent record (about 65% probability). From the Q2 2027 report (mid-July 2027) the cues themselves are likely to change character: management will have to speak to the hardening comparisons, to the end of the refill if it is ending, and to Data Center's run-rate after the Q3 2026 timing effect reverses, so the probability of a "reduced" cue (a warning on growth, mix or margin for the following quarter) rises to about 35-40% at each of the Q2 and Q3 2027 reports, against 10-15% at the next two. On the 2028 targets the asymmetry runs the other way: the Data Center target (SEK 3bn at 15%) is on course to be met a year early on the current run-rate (rolling twelve months above SEK 2bn; Q3 annualised about SEK 2.8bn) and is the only target that could be raised (about 30% within 18 months, most likely at a capital markets day setting 2030 targets rather than by lifting 2028); the Fiber Solutions target (SEK 6bn at 10%) will be "on track" on margin and short on sales unless acquisitions fill about SEK 0.3-0.4bn (held, about 60%; quietly missed on sales, 35%); the Harsh Environment target (SEK 2bn at 15%) is the one most likely to be missed (about 65%) and the one the company is least likely to cut explicitly (it will be left in place and explained by dynamic cables and the subsea timing); and the group 12% margin is most likely to be left in place and missed (about 60%), with the incentive plan's 9.8-11.1% range as the quiet acknowledgement. The one event that could change the whole picture is a capital markets day, which the files do not schedule; a new chief financial officer and a settled Data Center head make one likelier in H2 2027, and at that point the pattern since September 2025, destination targets that include acquisitions and no annual numbers, would probably be preserved, with 2030 targets replacing 2028 rather than 2028 being raised.

What drives each outcome, event by event.

  • 5 February 2027 (Q4 2026). Beat drivers: Data Center above SEK 550m (Redeye SEK 582m; the Data Center report finds a sustainable baseline around SEK 550m with Communication Zone and Superior lifting the floor); North American Fiber Solutions growth still above 20% (easy base, Clinton capacity retained); Fiber Solutions margin above 9% (Redeye 8.0%); submarine shipments as guided. Miss drivers: a glass or connector allocation cut turning backlog into lost revenue; a Data Center drop below SEK 500m; year-end charges (inventory reserves, earn-out revaluations). The consensus is four contributors, so the bar is where Redeye puts it: about SEK 2,254m of sales and a 9.6% margin.
  • Late April 2027 (Q1 2027). Beat drivers: the base (SEK 1,698m, 8.6% margin, organic -2%); seasonal Data Center recovery; first BEAD orders (Louisiana, Indiana, Pennsylvania are furthest along). Miss drivers: a cold winter again; a hyperscaler pause following the late-January capex guides; krona strength after a November Riksbank hike (a 5% stronger krona removes about 4.5% of reported revenue). Redeye models SEK 2,038m and SEK 217m; the second set SEK 2,090m and SEK 211m; a 20%-plus growth print is likely either way.
  • Mid-July and late October 2027 (Q2 and Q3 2027). These are the events at which guidance is most likely to be "reduced" in the only form it takes here, a cautious cue. Drivers: North American comparisons of +44% and +51%; Data Center's Q3 2026 timing benefit reversing (Q3 2027 flat year on year in the base case); Brightspeed's 2027 refinancing and AT&T's lower 2027-28 capex as signals for PE-funded and tier-1 demand; the first arrival of new fibre capacity (Chinese preform additions; G.652.D already falling) changing the pricing conversation. Beat drivers: BEAD orders ramping; supplier validation lifting the cap; Data Center sustaining SEK 650m-plus quarters. My base case has growth of 11.4% and 5.3% in these two quarters with margins of 11.1-11.4%, which would be "met" against today's cues and would still read as deceleration.
  • February and April 2028 (Q4 2027 and Q1 2028). Drivers: the 2028 targets come into their final year; the subsea line's commissioning status; whether the acquisition cadence has delivered SEK 400m-plus of 2027 deals. Probability of an explicit reset of the 2028 targets at the Q4 2027 report: about 25% (down for Harsh Environment, up or confirmed for Data Center).

The mid-term targets and ambitions, 18 months to three years out. Raised: Data Center only (30%); a 2030 framework at a capital markets day (40% that one is held by end-2027). Met: Data Center sales and margin (70%); Fiber Solutions margin (55%); group sales of SEK 11bn including acquisitions (45%, needing about SEK 1bn of bought revenue). Reduced or missed: Harsh Environment 15% (65% missed); group 12% (60% missed, landing at 11-11.5%); Fiber Solutions SEK 6bn (55% missed on sales). The second set's base case lands at SEK 10.8bn and 11.3% in 2028, inside the LTIP's upper half, which is where I expect the company itself to land its narrative: "sales on track with acquisitions; margin improving toward target."

How our assessment compares with guidance, and so the favourability of the gap. Near term, our assessment is above the cues (we expect them to be beaten at the next two events), which is favourable. Mid term, our assessment is below the headline target on margin and on Harsh Environment, which would be unfavourable if the market credited the target, but the one visible broker does not (Redeye's 2028 revenue SEK 10,213m; margin about 11.7%) and the incentive range signals that the board does not either, so the practical gap between what guidance says and what we expect is small and already half-priced. The favourable element is that the company's conservatism on cues gives the next two events a high probability of positive surprise against a consensus that is only four names wide.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0247
I-02

The likelihood that consensus forecasts and the net breadth of revisions move higher or lower over the next few earnings events, and for 18 months to three years out

❌ D47

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement. Consensus for Hexatronic is, in substance, one model (Redeye on FactSet; four contributors to the company-commissioned consensus, with SpareBank 1 the second identifiable house), so the questions of level and breadth collapse into one: what will Redeye and the three or four others do after each report. The sequence I expect is an upgrade that has already happened but is not yet in the data, a second small upgrade after the Q4 2026 report, a third after Q1 2027, and then a stall and a downside risk from the Q2 2027 report, with 2029 cut whenever a second broker publishes or the single broker rolls its model. Specifically: FactSet's weekly means (FY2026 SEK 2.26; FY2027 SEK 2.67; FY2028 SEK 3.07 at 16 October) will move to about SEK 2.39, 2.85 and 3.36 as Redeye's 23 October numbers are absorbed, a mechanical +5% to +9% that the dashboard will show as a one-month revision burst in November; after the Q4 2026 report, FY2027 moves up another 2-4% if Data Center lands above SEK 550m and Fiber Solutions holds above 9% (probability about 60%), and is held otherwise; after Q1 2027, FY2027 is nudged up again on the easy comparison (about 55%); from the Q2 2027 report the probability of a cut to FY2027 and FY2028 rises to about 40% at each of the next two events, because the comparisons harden, the refill question is answered and the supply cap is binding, while the probability of an upgrade falls to about 25%. By the end of the 18 months (April 2028) I expect FY2027 EPS consensus to have ended within ±5% of SEK 2.85 (our SEK 2.78 is 2.5% below Redeye's post-Q3 figure, so we are not forecasting an upgrade cycle from here), FY2028 to be about SEK 3.2-3.4 (ours SEK 3.29, in line), and FY2029, which rests on one broker's SEK 4.18 with a 12.1% EBIT margin and falling depreciation, to be cut toward SEK 3.6-3.8 (ours SEK 3.58) once anyone else models it or Redeye extends its model with the capacity cycle in view. Breadth is almost meaningless at four names and one visible; what matters is whether a second or third house appears on FactSet, which is likelier after a capital markets day or the CFO appointment than after any single quarter.

The arithmetic of where consensus now sits against us.

FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029
FactSet median at 22 October (pre-Q3) EPS 2.30 2.71 3.23 4.18
Redeye, 23 October (post-Q3) EPS 2.39 2.85 3.36 not stated
Our cross-check EPS (reported basis) 2.38 2.78 3.29 3.58
Ours against Redeye post-Q3 -0.4% -2.5% -2.1% about -14% against the FactSet one-broker figure
Revenue: Redeye post-Q3 / ours (SEKm) not stated / 8,670 9,631 / 9,670 10,213 / 10,780 not stated / 11,890
Adjusted EBITA margin: Redeye / ours about 10.4-10.5% / 10.1% about 11% / 10.9% about 11.7% / 11.3% n.a. / 10.9%

The reading is that after the Q3 upgrade the one visible broker is at or slightly above our base case for 2026-28 on EPS, slightly above on margin and below on revenue (because it excludes unannounced acquisitions and we include SEK 1.1bn by 2028 at 12% margins). The pre-Q3 FactSet medians that the model summary compares us with are stale; the summary's "+14%, +23%, +21%, +2%" EPS gaps against consensus are an artefact of the pre-amortisation basis and the revenue base (D-01), and on a like-for-like, post-Q3 basis we are not above consensus for 2026-28 at all. The favourable gap for the case therefore lies in the quarterly path (we and Redeye both expect beats of the four-contributor consensus at the next two prints, because the company sets the bar) rather than in the annual level, and the unfavourable gap lies in 2029 and in the margin trajectory beyond 2028, where the single-broker figure embeds a margin step we do not expect.

What moves estimates up, and what moves them down, over the 18 months. Up: Data Center above SEK 600m in any quarter (the upside scenario's 25% organic growth); supplier validation easing the cap in H1 2027; BEAD orders from mid-2027; a Fiber Solutions margin above 10% on North American volume; a larger acquisition (the SEK 400m block is partly in our numbers and not in Redeye's); a weaker krona. Down: North American growth in single digits before the comparisons harden; a hyperscaler financing event (the CoreWeave and Oracle signals of October 2026); a Data Center quarter below SEK 500m; a glass or connector allocation cut disclosed as lost revenue; a stronger krona after a Riksbank hike; a defensive European consolidation with SEK 100-150m of one-offs; and, for 2029 alone, the mere act of another broker modelling the capacity cycle. The two sector facts most likely to move Hexatronic's consensus without any Hexatronic news are Corning's and Prysmian's quarterly commentary (own-4 finds Corning's shipment commentary leads Hexatronic's cycle by about two quarters) and hyperscaler 2027 capex guidance in late January and early February 2027.

Breadth, revision momentum and the dashboard. The dashboard's pinned sell-side momentum rating is C (revision score 11; one-month and three-month EPS revisions nil; six-month +4%; twelve-month +16%; target-price six-month +36%; buy share 75% after a 50-point rise over six months). When FactSet absorbs Redeye's update the one-month figure will print about +5% to +9% for FY2026-27 and the rating would improve, and the mean target price (SEK 48.60 against a price of SEK 40.94; Redeye's base case SEK 46) would hold. That is a momentum signal the dashboard will register in November, and the memo Analyst should understand that it reflects the Q3 print, not new information. The HMS Networks precedent in the guidance report (16 of 18 quarters beating consensus margins, and a sharp consensus valley during its 2024 destock) is the right template for how a thin Nordic consensus behaves: it follows the prints, it does not anticipate them, and it swings hard when the cycle turns.

18 months to three years out. FY2028 consensus is likely to be held within ±5% (ours in line). FY2029 is likely to be cut by 10-15% to a figure near ours, but the timing is unknowable because it depends on a second broker; until then the FactSet 2029 figure overstates what "the market" expects and the memo should not use it as the market's view. If a capital markets day sets 2030 targets, consensus for 2029-30 will be rebuilt around them; our SEK 3.58 and SEK 3.98 assume no margin step and the capacity cycle trimming margins by 30-50 basis points, which is below where a company-anchored consensus would start.

The favourability of the gap. Near term (to April 2027) favourable: two likely beats of a bar the company sets, a mechanical revision burst in November, and a consensus that moves only after prints. Twelve to eighteen months out, neutral to unfavourable: consensus already at our level for 2027-28, the second-half 2027 comparisons likely to produce the first cautious cue and a 40% chance of cuts at each of two events, and a 2029 figure that will come down. Net, the sell-side gap is slightly unfavourable for a case that depends on upgrades from here, and favourable only for a case that depends on the next two prints.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0342
I-03

How comprehensively the dynamics are articulated in sell-side and investor commentary, and how well known each is

❌ D42

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement. The narrative has moved from "busted FTTH duct maker" to "data-centre and US fibre recovery story" about two thirds of the way, and the dynamics that have carried the share price (the Fiber Solutions margin recovery, Data Center's doubling, the US rebound, the balance-sheet repair, the acquisition programme, the subsea partnership) are fully articulated by the one active research house, by the company's own communication and by the Nordic forums in a fragmented way. The dynamics that will decide 2027 and that our assessment weights most are articulated thinly or not at all: of the thirteen dynamics mapped by the narrative report (t-i2), five are widely discussed (the Fiber Solutions recovery, Data Center's Q4 normalisation, the supply constraint as a growth limiter, the equity-funded acquisition loop and the subsea doubling), four are discussed by one house only and briefly (services dilution of Data Center margin, the hardening North American comparisons, the leadership vacancies, the Harsh Environment margin gap), and four are discussed by no one (the LTIP 2026 range of 9.8-11.1% against the 12% target; the consensus EPS basis and the SEK 67m reversal inside the FY2026 figure; the two-way effect of the 2027-28 capacity arrival on a non-integrated assembler; the acquired-working-capital and depreciation-dilution points that flatter cash conversion and margin). The refill-versus-use question for the US recovery is posed by Redeye and by expert calls but decomposed by nobody; the negative skew of 2027 and the 2029 consensus artefact are our findings alone. The sell-side narrative treats the recovery as predominantly structural (the SEK 120m programme; diversification; "less cyclical than FTTH"), treats the supply constraint as damping rather than threatening, and treats services as not diluting because Q3 beat; the investor narrative on Avanza Placera, Börssnack and X is episodic and price-led rather than analytical. Where our assessment is more positive than the narrative (two likely beats; a structurally higher Fiber Solutions floor; Data Center's leverage higher because services are 39% not half; G.652.D already falling), the gap is small and partly priced by a share that has risen 113% in twelve months to its ten-year median multiple. Where our assessment is more negative (the 2027 skew; the margin plateau at 11% against 12%; the 2029 figure; US duct price pressure; the Hudiksvall slip; the single-project concentration in Data Center), the narrative does not carry it, which means those risks are not priced and would arrive as surprises. For an investment case the balance is unfavourable: the parts of our view that the market does not know are mostly the cautious ones.

The thirteen dynamics, who discusses each, and how it compares with our view.

  1. Fiber Solutions recovery, structural or cyclical. Redeye (sequential improvement, programme "instrumental", margin "close to the 10% target"; but also, since October 2024, that the US duct market is "unlikely to match 2021-23 peaks"); management; forums as a margin story. Widely discussed; the structural reading dominates. Our view: about half structural (the cost base) and half cyclical (US absorption against duct price pressure). Gap: unfavourable, the market is more structural than we are.
  2. Services dilution of Data Center margin. Redeye noted Superior's 12.5% margin (May 2026) and the Q2 2026 margin fall to 15.4%, then concluded in October that Q3 "beat dilution expectations". One house, resolved optimistically. Our view: dilution is slow (services 39%) and the real issue is product-timing volatility. Gap: roughly neutral.
  3. Data Center normalisation and sustainable level. Management flagged it; Redeye models SEK 582m for Q4 and SEK 536m for Q1 2027; the Data Center report finds a baseline about SEK 550m. Widely discussed. Our view: SEK 550m. Gap: neutral; the known risk is the single Communication Zone project (Redeye mentions it; forums do not).
  4. Hardening North American comparisons and refill against use. Redeye notes the "demanding hurdle for mid-2027" and models Q2 2027 growth of 14%; expert calls raise restocking; no decomposition. One house, lightly. Our view: the central 2027 risk. Gap: unfavourable.
  5. Supply constraint. Management disclosed it; Redeye: "dampen near-term growth rather than threaten"; Jefferies and TrendForce at sector level. Widely discussed as a limiter, not as a working-capital or allocation risk. Our view: caps 2027 organic growth at 9%; binds Data Center products more than ducts. Gap: neutral.
  6. Capacity arriving in 2027-28. Jefferies, Nuvama, Huatai, Morgan Stanley at sector level; Redeye only as "more supply in 2027" (a positive). Nobody models the two-way effect for Hexatronic. Our view: lower input costs first, lost scarcity pricing and deeper European price pressure second, a 30-50 basis-point margin drift in 2029-30. Gap: unfavourable for 2029-30 expectations.
  7. Acquisition-funding loop, the architect's departure, private-equity competition. Redeye positive on the equity issue; Åberg's departure noted in press releases and by Redeye as a key-person loss; private-equity competition raised by Redeye in 2025. Widely discussed, benignly. Our view: the loop holds while the share stays above about 9-10x; US installer multiples (6.9-9.7x) are above the stated range. Gap: slightly unfavourable.
  8. LTIP range against the 12% target. Nobody. Our view: the board's own planning anchor is 9.8-11.1%. Gap: unfavourable (the market is still shown 12%).
  9. Consensus EPS basis and the SEK 67m reversal. Nobody. Redeye's SEK 2.39 includes about SEK 0.23 of reversal. Our view: clean 2026 EPS about SEK 2.20. Gap: unfavourable (the 2026 base is overstated, so 2027 growth is understated in the narrative, which cuts both ways).
  10. Working capital, cash conversion, no dividend. Redeye notes the inventory build and the acquisitions-first policy; forums note the absence of a dividend. Discussed. Our view: free cash flow after leases covers the acquisition cadence with little left until 2028. Gap: neutral.
  11. Hudiksvall and Harsh Environment margin gap. Redeye: "grind slowly" toward 15%; subsea revenue from 2029; permit slip visible only in the annual report. One house. Our view: 13% in 2028, 14.5% by 2030, subsea at risk of a one-year slip. Gap: neutral to slightly unfavourable.
  12. Krona, small-cap, thin coverage, no anchor owner. Management on the krona; Redeye on register and liquidity. Discussed. Our view: a 25-40% drawdown on intact earnings is plausible. Gap: neutral.
  13. Leadership vacancies. Press releases; Redeye notes acting roles; little investor attention. One house. Our view: the February 2027 test. Gap: slightly unfavourable.

The peers' narratives and what investors in the sector are focused on. The cohort's narrative in October 2026 is AI-monetisation anxiety and the sustainability of hyperscaler capex (Corning's July sell-off; the optical cohort -4.5% over three months; CoreWeave and Oracle credit signals); the US fibre narrative is BEAD timing and tier-1 capex discipline (AT&T's lower 2027-28 capex; Tier-1 carriers moving 15-20% of fibre spend to lower-cost suppliers); the European cable narrative is subsea backlogs and capacity (NKT, Nexans, Prysmian); the Nordic small-cap narrative is HMS Networks' serial-acquirer template, with Hexatronic increasingly discussed in the same frame. Investors in the sector are focused on whether AI capex pauses and on when glass scarcity ends; they are not focused on US duct pricing, on the Q2 2027 comparison cliff or on the incentive range, which are the Hexatronic-specific items that will matter in 2027.

Coverage and the quality of the evidence. Active research: Redeye (the only house with a published model and quarterly notes) and SpareBank 1; four contributors to the company's consensus; Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, Morgan Stanley and Bernstein cover the sector and peers but not Hexatronic (Deutsche Bank mentioned it in index notes in 2023). Investor commentary on Swedish forums and X is described by the narrative report as fragmented and episodic; the report found no sustained analytical thread on any of the thirteen dynamics. The memo should therefore say that "the narrative" for this stock is one broker, the company and price action, and that the gap between our assessment and the narrative is as much a gap in articulation as a gap in view.

Primary horizon (to April 2028) and secondary (18 to 36 months). Primary: the positive near-term dynamics are known (Redeye expects Q1 2027 to beat; the Clinton reversal is read as proof of demand), the negative mid-2027 dynamics are known in outline (the comparison hurdle) and not in consequence (no downside scenario is published); the gap is unfavourable because the surprises that remain are mostly negative. Secondary: the subsea step and the defence tailwind are known; the capacity cycle's margin effect, the 2029 consensus artefact and the margin target gap are not; the gap is unfavourable for expectations beyond 2028 and mildly favourable only in that a market that eventually accepts an 11% through-cycle margin on SEK 11-12bn of sales may still find today's multiple reasonable.

Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Changed forecast inputs: Hexatronic Group AB (HTRO-SE), from the third and fourth sets, as at 25-Oct-26

Money in millions of SEK, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the line below changes; every other line in Parts 1 to 3 of the second set's forecast inputs stands.

Note for the Financial Analyst before applying the change: the model's FY2026 revenue (SEK 8,986.5m, "+24.0%") is about 3.6% above the second set's company-basis figure (SEK 8,670m) because the Part 3 quarterly growth rates were applied to FactSet's quarterly history (Q3 2025 SEK 1,981m and Q4 2025 SEK 1,999m) rather than to the company's reported quarters (SEK 1,883m and SEK 1,848m); the error compounds into every later year. No growth input is wrong; the quarterly base should be rebased to the company's figures (or FY2026 built from the Part 1 rate of 15.3% on the company's FY2025 revenue of SEK 7,519m) before the memo is written. The change below is independent of that correction.

Part 2. Cash flow and balance sheet inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Working capital (% of revenue) 22.5 22.0 21.5 21.0 21.0

The model treats the whole change in the working-capital balance as an operating cash outflow, which charges the receivables and inventories that arrive with acquired companies (about SEK 130m in 2026 for JOWO and Superior, about SEK 100m a year thereafter on SEK 450m of acquired revenue) a second time on top of the acquisition line, so the ratio is set 1.5 to 2.0 points below the expected year-end balance-sheet ratio (24.0, 24.0, 23.5, 23.0, 23.0) to bring the model's free cash flow back into line with the judgement (about SEK 420-460m before acquisitions in 2026 and about SEK 400-450m in 2027); if the model is instead changed to carry acquired working capital inside the acquisition line, restore the second set's path.

Working capital is the balance at the end of each fiscal year as a percentage of the last twelve months' revenue.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of HTRO-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Hexatronic Group AB (HTRO-SE; Nasdaq Stockholm), a Swedish maker of passive fibre-optic infrastructure with three segments: Fiber Solutions (microducts, ducts, air-blown fibre cables, connectivity and submarine fibre cable for FTTH, transport and middle-mile networks in Europe, North America and Asia-Pacific; about 57% of Q3 2026 sales), Data Center (high-density cabling and connectivity plus design, installation and integration services for hyperscale, colocation and enterprise data centres, mainly in the United States; about 28%) and Harsh Environment (dynamic cables, specialist connectors and fibre sensing for defence, offshore energy and marine; about 16%); North America was 48% of Q3 2026 sales, Europe 43%, Asia-Pacific 9%.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Hexatronic Group AB (HTRO-SE), whose North American Fiber Solutions sales grew 44% in Q2 2026 and 51% in Q3 2026 and whose US product range is duct and microduct made at Clinton (South Carolina) and Ogden (Utah) until its US fibre-cable line is commissioned in 2027, what has happened to US HDPE conduit and microduct pricing, capacity and utilisation in 2025 and 2026 (Orbia's Dura-Line, which reported Q2 2026 Connectivity Solutions sales up 30% with conduit pricing under pressure from domestic overcapacity, and again cited conduit price pressure in its Q3 2026 reporting; Atkore; Southwire; other domestic extruders), which price increases Hexatronic announced in the United States in 2026 and for which products (duct, microduct or fibre cable), how large the Build America Buy America compliance premium for domestic duct has been in awarded BEAD and other subsidised projects, what distributors (Graybar, Wesco, Anixter) and private-equity-backed tier-2 and tier-3 builders say about 2027 conduit pricing and volumes, and how, in the comparable second year after the 2012-13 and 2023-24 US destocks, duct and conduit makers' volumes and prices moved; state near the top how many sources and companies were found and used, date every piece of evidence, separate what the evidence shows from inference, and conclude with the hypotheses the evidence supports and the indicators that would decide the question.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Hexatronic Group AB (HTRO-SE), whose board adopted LTIP 2026 at the extraordinary general meeting of 26 August 2026 (notice of 22 July 2026) with performance criteria weighted 40% diluted earnings per share, 25% sales growth, 25% EBITA margin (vesting on an average EBITA margin of 9.8% to 11.1% over 2026-28) and 10% sustainability, and whose 2028 financial targets (set September 2025 and May 2026, acquisitions included) are group net sales of SEK 11bn and an EBITA margin of about 12%, what diluted EPS and sales-growth thresholds does LTIP 2026 set for minimum and maximum vesting over 2026-28, how do they and the earlier plans' thresholds (LTIP 2024 and LTIP 2025) compare with the published targets and with the covering brokers' forecasts (Redeye, 23 October 2026, diluted EPS of SEK 2.39 for 2026 and SEK 3.36 for 2028; SpareBank 1), what did the board and the nomination committee (Accendo Capital's representative) say to institutional holders when the plan was rebased to a three-year average, how have comparable Nordic companies' incentive ranges related to their published targets and outcomes (HMS Networks, Lagercrantz, Lifco, Invisio, Kitron, Nolato), whether Hexatronic has announced or been asked about a capital markets day or revised targets for 2027 or 2028, and what is publicly known about the recruitment of a permanent chief financial officer and a permanent head of the Data Center segment (both vacant since the executive changes announced on 1 September 2026 and 15 July 2026); date every piece of evidence, separate what the evidence shows from inference, and conclude with the hypotheses the evidence supports and the indicators that would decide the question.

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Hexatronic Group AB (HTRO-SE), whose shares rose from about SEK 16-17 in early 2025 to SEK 40.94 on 22 October 2026 and whose 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple on FactSet consensus stood at about 13.4x on 16 October 2026 (ten-year median about 13.6x; 2021-22 peak above 25x; 2024-25 trough about 5x), how did the forward price-to-earnings multiples and share prices of comparable small- and mid-cap fibre, cable, connectivity and Nordic hardware companies behave in the second year after a destock-driven earnings recovery, when did the multiple peak relative to the peak in consensus earnings revisions and relative to the first quarter of decelerating organic growth, and how far did the multiple fall before earnings fell: cover Hexatronic's own 2021-23 cycle (consensus FY2026 EPS from SEK 5.05 at its peak to SEK 1.76 at its trough), Clearfield (fiscal 2022-24), Dura-Line and Orbia (2021-25), Corning Optical Communications (2022-24 and 2025-26), Nokia and Adtran (optical cycles), HMS Networks (2022-25), Kitron, Invisio, Nexans and NKT (the 2016-20 high-voltage cable cycle and its reversal), and Prysmian (telecom cycle 2018-21); state which valuation bands (relative to each stock's own history) proved sustainable once growth decelerated and which did not, how thin coverage and low free float affected the speed of the de-rating, and what observable indicators (revision breadth, order commentary, peer guidance, spot input prices) preceded the multiple's turn by how many months; date every piece of evidence, separate what the evidence shows from inference, and conclude with the hypotheses the evidence supports and the indicators that would decide the question.
